# AVRL research ledger

Entries are appended by the autoresearch loop and by hand. Verdicts are against the pre-registered thresholds in PROJECT.md and EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md.

## E01 2026-09-30 kill (for claim 3 on this task/proxy pair)
Hypothesis: proxy error (group majority answer wrong) has exploitable structure in prompt space and is not already captured by cheap features.
Run: Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, GSM8K train, 3,000 prompts x 8 rollouts (vLLM, temperature 1.0), proxy = agreement with group majority, verifier = gold answer. Files: runs/p0/summary.json, result.json, feats.parquet, sim_*.json, analyze.log.

| quantity | value |
|---|---|
| rollout accuracy / majority accuracy | 64.2% / 85.5% |
| group proxy-error rate (majority wrong) | 14.5% |
| proxy error, unanimous groups (18.6% of groups) | 0.5% |
| proxy error, split groups | 17.7% |
| AUC for proxy error: agreement only / all cheap / embedding only / cheap + embedding | 0.914 / 0.923 / 0.606 / 0.922 |
| neighbor ratio of proxy error by embedding cell (k = 30 / 100 / 300) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 |
| confident subset (agreement >= 0.75, n = 1,551): error rate / cheap AUC / neighbor ratio | 1.1% / 0.63 / 1.16 |

Allocation replay (errors caught per budget, 100 cells, 8 seeds; columns: uniform, static with free labels [approximately the agreement threshold], static budget-matched, TS flat, greedy online self-trained, TS online, TS + free prior, neighbor, oracle):
10%: 28, 165, 98, 31, 138, 95, 118, 53, 218. 25%: 76, 264, 212, 83, 253, 208, 232, 117, 309. 50%: 157, 299, 287, 164, 296, 288, 294, 206, 309. Embedding features change nothing.

Verdict: pre-registered read in PROJECT.md triggers (embeddings add < 0.05 AUC, neighbor ratio < 1.5): claim 3 (edge from prompt-space structure) is dead on GSM8K with a majority-vote proxy. The fixed agreement threshold is near-optimal here; the self-training allocator matches it at 25% and 50% budgets and loses at 10%. This is the "threshold regime" and becomes the first point of the structure-statistic-versus-gain curve (see the revised proposal): cheap-feature AUC 0.92, gain of learned over threshold about 0.
What it changes: the allocator can only earn its keep where the proxy lacks a confidence signal. Next: E04 (small judge model as proxy, no self-consistency), E05 (self-verification proxy), E08 (code task where majority vote over programs is ill-defined), each reporting the same three statistics plus the replay, to populate the curve. Also add an explicit label-free agreement-threshold policy to the replay so the baseline is not approximated by "static with free labels".

## E03 2026-09-30 kill
Hypothesis: at the budget phase-1 plans to spend (25%), the learned allocator (ts_online analog) admits fewer corrupted-reward groups than the threshold allocator (static analog) by a margin that survives resampling noise, not just as a point estimate.
Run: no new rollouts, CPU-only (sklearn/pandas), reuses avrl/runs/p0/feats.parquet (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, GSM8K, majority-vote proxy, n=3,000 groups, same population as E01). group_col=cell100 (75 buckets after MIN=3 filter), budget_frac=0.25, 500 bootstrap resamples of groups (seed 0, 0 degenerate), 8 seeds per policy call inside each resample (matches p0's SEEDS=8). Policies, mapped per the E03 plan's explicit assumption since sim_generic.py's names don't literally match allocators.py's phase-1 arm names: "static" (threshold analog: classifier fit once on a free-labeled held-out quarter of cell100 clusters, deterministic ranking) vs "ts_online" (learned analog: Thompson sampling over cells, online-refit every R verified labels, no free labels). Files: avrl/exp/e03_bootstrap_sig.py, avrl/runs/E03/result.json.

| quantity | value |
|---|---|
| point estimate hits, static / ts_online (of 309 corrupted groups in sim pop) | 264 / 207.9 |
| threshold_corrupted_admitted_rate / learned_corrupted_admitted_rate | 14.6% / 32.7% |
| point diff (threshold minus learned) | -0.182 |
| bootstrap 95% CI on diff (500 resamples) | [-0.225, -0.114] |
| bootstrap mean / sd | -0.172 / 0.028 |

Verdict: kill (for claim 2, at this budget, under E03's policy mapping). Script's own verdict label: KILL_REVERSED (CI excludes zero and is entirely negative), pass=false. This is not the pre-registered "kill by noise" case (CI including zero) -- it is a significant result in the direction opposite to what claim 2 requires: static/threshold admits fewer corrupted-reward groups than ts_online/learned, with high confidence. Cross-checked the point estimate against runs/p0/analyze.log's cell100/cheap 25%-budget row by reading sim_alloc.py's actual print() column order in source (not just the markdown header, which is easy to misalign against): uniform=76, static=264, static_matched=212, ts_flat=83, greedy_online=253, ts_online=208, ts_static=232, neighbor=117, oracle=309. E03's hits_static=264 and hits_ts_online=207.9 match exactly, confirming this is a real, reproducible reading and not a bootstrap-code artifact. Caveat (flagged in the plan and repeated in result.json's own note): "static" gets a free labeled quarter of cell100 clusters beyond the 25% audit budget that "ts_online" never gets, so this is not a budget-matched comparison -- it structurally favors static. sim_generic.py's existing "static_matched" policy (budget-matched, no free labels) was available and not used here, per the plan's literal static/ts_online mapping.

What it changes: combined with E01's clean double kill of claim 3 (embeddings add ~0 AUC, confident-subset proxy error 1.1% under the 3% bar, neighbor ratio 1.16x under 1.5x), claim 2 is now also unsupported on this exact config: the "learned" allocator (Thompson-sampling, online-refit) does not beat a fixed threshold at the budget phase-1 intends to spend, it loses, significantly. Only claim 1 (same policy quality, fewer expensive calls than full verification) remains untested, and is the weakest of the three claims since a naive fixed rule likely also satisfies it. This argues against funding E15's hannah GRPO run on current evidence, pending the budget-matched re-check below (the free-label asymmetry means this result is not yet the last word on claim 2).

Follow-ups:
1. (L3a, ~15 min, CPU) Rerun the identical E03 bootstrap swapping "static" for sim_generic.py's existing "static_matched" policy (budget-matched, no free labels) as the threshold analog, keeping "ts_online" as learned. Directly tests whether E03's reversal is caused by static's free-label advantage or is a real effect. Cheapest, most decision-relevant next check: static_matched already exists in the harness, no new allocator class needed (unlike G01's recalibrated threshold, which requires building one).
2. (L3b, ~15 min, CPU) Rerun E03's bootstrap at budget_frac=0.10 and 0.50 (E02's budget grid) to see if the threshold-beats-learned reversal is budget-dependent. E01's analyze.log point estimates hint the gap widens at 10% (165 static vs 95 ts_online) and narrows at 50% (299 vs 288), so a budget regime might exist where learned stops losing.
3. (L3c, larger effort, needs scoping) Rerun the same bootstrap significance test using allocators.py's actual LearnedAllocator and a literal fixed-threshold class (not sim_generic.py's static/ts_online structural analogs) against feats.parquet offline, to check whether the real phase-1 code reproduces E03's reversal or whether the analogy chosen here was misleading.

## L3a 2026-09-30 kill

Hypothesis: E03's KILL_REVERSED result (static/threshold admits significantly fewer corrupted-reward groups than ts_online/learned at 25% budget, bootstrap 95% CI [-0.225, -0.114]) is driven by an acknowledged confound: E03's "static" policy gets a free-labeled quarter of cell100 clusters beyond the 25% audit budget that "ts_online" never gets. Swapping in sim_generic.py's existing static_matched policy (budget-matched: spends half the audit budget on random labels, fits, ranks the rest, no labels beyond budget) as the threshold analog, with ts_online unchanged, tests whether the reversal survives once the comparison is budget-matched.

Run: no new rollouts, CPU-only (sklearn/pandas), reuses avrl/runs/p0/feats.parquet (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, GSM8K, majority-vote proxy, n=3,000 groups, same population as E01/E03). group_col=cell100, budget_frac=0.25, 500 bootstrap resamples of groups (seed 0, 0 skipped as degenerate), 8 seeds per policy call inside each resample (matches E03/p0's SEEDS=8). Script: avrl/exp/e03b_bootstrap_matched.py, copied from avrl/exp/e03_bootstrap_sig.py per the project rule (never edit p0/E03 scripts in place). Diffed the two scripts directly: only the "static" branch of fit_eval was replaced with sim_generic.py's static_matched logic (verified against runs/sim_generic.py's own static_matched branch, verbatim match); the ts_online loop, corrupted_admitted_rate definition, and the bootstrap procedure are byte-identical to E03. Files: avrl/exp/e03b_bootstrap_matched.py, avrl/runs/L3a/result.json, run.log, done.flag, exit_code (0). Runtime 112.8s (cap was 45 min).

| quantity | E03 (static, free-labeled) | L3a (static_matched, budget-matched) |
|---|---|---|
| point estimate hits, threshold-analog / ts_online (of 309 corrupted groups) | 264 / 207.9 | 212.0 / 207.9 |
| threshold-analog_corrupted_admitted_rate / learned_corrupted_admitted_rate | 14.6% / 32.7% | 31.4% / 32.7% |
| point diff (threshold-analog minus learned) | -0.182 | -0.0133 |
| bootstrap 95% CI on diff (500 resamples) | [-0.225, -0.114] | [-0.0568, 0.0348] |
| bootstrap mean / sd | -0.172 / 0.028 | -0.0102 / 0.0242 |
| script's own verdict label | KILL_REVERSED | KILL |

Verdict against L3a's pre-registered thresholds: kill. Pass required the CI to exclude zero and be positive (static_matched significantly beats ts_online once budget-matched, supporting claim 2 and explaining away E03's reversal as a confound). Kill required either the CI excluding zero and negative (reversal survives budget-matching, it is real) or the CI including zero (no significant difference at this budget once matched). The observed CI [-0.0568, 0.0348] includes zero, so this is the second kill case: not distinguishable from resampling noise. Numbers were cross-checked directly from avrl/runs/L3a/result.json (not just the run report): rates recompute exactly from (309 - hits)/309 for both arms, and the e03 vs e03b script diff confirms only the static branch changed.

What it changes: E03's free-label confound is confirmed as (at least partly) responsible for the earlier reversal. Once the threshold analog is budget-matched, its point-estimate advantage over ts_online collapses from -0.182 to -0.0133, and the bootstrap CI no longer excludes zero. So the strong claim "learned loses to threshold" (E03's literal result) does not survive budget-matching. But this does not rescue claim 2 either: the pass condition (learned significantly beats a fair threshold) was also not met. At 25% budget on cell100/GSM8K/majority-vote, static_matched and ts_online are statistically indistinguishable. Combined with E01's clean kill of claim 3, the evidence for claim 2 is now "no significant edge in either direction" rather than "learned loses" -- still insufficient to justify funding E15's hannah GRPO run on current evidence, since claim 2 as stated in PROJECT.md requires that learned beats a fair threshold, not merely ties it.

Follow-ups:
1. (L4a, ~30 min total, CPU) Rerun L3a's exact script (e03b_bootstrap_matched.py, static_matched vs ts_online) at budget_frac=0.10 and 0.50, the same grid E01/E03's follow-up L3b proposed but using the validated budget-matched baseline instead of the free-label "static" L3b still plans to use. Tests whether the 25%-budget null is budget-dependent, and supersedes L3b's baseline choice now that static's free-label advantage is a known confound.
2. (L4b, ~15 min, CPU) Rerun L3a with the per-resample policy seed count raised from 8 to 32 (only SEEDS changed, everything else byte-identical) to shrink Monte Carlo noise inside each bootstrap resample. Distinguishes "genuinely tied at this budget" from "underpowered to detect a small true effect" -- the current CI is about 0.09 wide against a point diff of -0.013, so a chunk of that width could be seed noise rather than group-resampling noise.
3. (L4c, ~20 min, CPU) Rerun L3a's script with --group cell30 and --group cell300 (both columns already exist in feats.parquet, confirmed by reading it directly) instead of cell100. Tests whether the null result is sensitive to the audit-group granularity, since phase-1's actual grouping choice is not guaranteed to match cell100 and a coarser or finer grouping changes both the per-bucket budget and the classifier's fitting power.

## E04 2026-09-30 kill

Hypothesis: a small LLM judge's error pattern (which rollouts it mis-scores, given only the question and the extracted final answer, no gold) is structured in prompt-embedding space similarly to majority-vote's error pattern (E01), but the two proxies are wrong on different, only partially overlapping subsets of prompts.

Run: judge model Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct (vLLM, temperature 0, max_tokens 6, gpu_memory_utilization 0.85), no new policy generation, reuses E01's Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct rollouts on GSM8K (avrl/runs/p0/rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet, feats.parquet: 3,000 prompts x 8 rollouts). Judge scored the 10,168 unique (pid, extracted-answer) pairs (deduplicated from 24,000 rollouts), prompted with question + proposed answer only, yes/no. Per group, judge_pick = distinct answer with the highest judge "yes"-weighted self-consistency count, ties broken toward higher count then answer string; groups where the judge said "no" to everything fall back to the group's own majority answer by construction (judge_all_no_frac = 0.0, never triggered). Scripts: avrl/exp/e04_judge_score.py, avrl/exp/e04_structure.py (both new, do not edit p0 scripts). Files: avrl/runs/E04/judge_scores.parquet, judge.log, result.json.

One real bug hit and fixed before this ran: the script's initial `gpu_memory_utilization=0.5` left vLLM's v0.19 CUDA-graph memory profiler with negative KV-cache headroom (`ValueError: No available memory for the cache blocks. Try increasing gpu_memory_utilization...`, `Available KV cache memory: -1.95 GiB`) and the engine failed to start. Raised to 0.85 (matching p0_rollouts.py's working value) and it ran clean; full traceback was in avrl/runs/E04/judge.log before the fix, now overwritten by the successful run's log.

| quantity | E01 (majority-vote proxy) | E04 (judge proxy) |
|---|---|---|
| proxy/judge error rate | 14.5% | 14.4% |
| confident-subset error rate (share >= 0.75, n = 1,551) | 1.1% | 1.1% |
| AUC cheap / cheap+emb / emb-only | 0.923 / 0.922 / 0.606 | 0.922 / 0.921 / 0.606 |
| AUC cheap+emb minus cheap | -0.0007 (from E01's own numbers) | -0.0006 |
| neighbor ratio (cell30/100/300) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 | 1.00 / 1.04 / 1.04 |
| Jaccard(majority-wrong, judge-wrong) | n/a | 0.993 (433 intersection / 436 union) |
| judge_pick == majority answer | n/a | 99.27% of groups (2,978/3,000) |

Verdict against pre-registered thresholds: KILL. Pass required auc_cheap_emb - auc_cheap >= 0.05 AND Jaccard < 0.5; kill required Jaccard > 0.8 or fewer than 10 judge_error=1 groups. Observed auc gap is -0.0006 (no structure gain from embeddings, same as E01) and Jaccard is 0.993, far past the kill line. result.json's own verdict field agrees (KILL, pass=false, kill=true).

Root cause, checked directly (not just trusting the aggregate number): judge_pick equals the majority answer in 2,978 of 3,000 groups (99.27%). Cross-tab of judge_error against majority's own proxy_error shows all 433 judge errors are also majority errors, judge is correct on 3 of majority's 436 errors, and wrong on zero cases majority got right. This is a mechanism artifact, not evidence that the judge is a good verifier: the tally rule (yes-vote weight = self-consistency count if judge says "yes", tie broken by that same count) means that whenever the judge says "yes" to the majority answer, which the log shows it does in the overwhelming majority of cases, the majority answer wins regardless of what it says about other candidates. Of the 22 groups where judge_pick differed from the majority answer, 19 of those were cases where the majority's own extracted answer was "0" (an extraction-failure placeholder, not a real majority), so the judge's "independent" signal barely surfaced anywhere in this run.

What it changes: claim 3 (proxy choice is itself a lever, not just budget) gets no support from this run, but the experiment as speced could not have distinguished "the judge genuinely has the same error pattern as majority vote" from "the tally mechanism collapses the judge into majority vote regardless of what the judge actually thinks." Both are live explanations and this run cannot separate them. E04's own pre-registered design (self-consistency-weighted tie-break) is the likely confound, not necessarily a real finding about judge-type proxies in general. This should be read as inconclusive-about-the-underlying-question even though it is a clean KILL against the literal pre-registered thresholds: proxy-type variation is not yet shown to be closed off, only this particular aggregation rule is shown to degenerate to majority vote.

Follow-ups:
1. (L5a, ~15 min, goldberg GPU) Extend e04_judge_score.py to also dump the per-(pid, answer) judge_yes column (same 10,168 pairs, same ~66s judge pass, no new prompt design), then rerun the group-level aggregation with a tie-break that does not use self-consistency count at all: e.g. random tie-break among judge-endorsed answers, or "first distinct answer encountered." Directly tests whether E04's Jaccard = 0.993 is a real finding or an artifact of the count-weighted tie-break; cheapest way to separate the two explanations flagged above.
2. (L5b, ~25 min, goldberg GPU) Run E05 (self-verification proxy, backlog id E05) next as planned: a mechanism with no self-consistency count in its design at all (the policy re-asked to check its own answer), so it cannot suffer from the same tally degeneracy and gives a cleaner second point on the proxy-type axis.
3. (L5c, ~5 min analysis-only, reuses already-judged pairs if L5a's log-saving change lands) Compute judge accuracy at the unique-(pid, answer) level directly (judge_yes vs. answer == gold, before any group-level aggregation or tie-break). This is the most direct diagnostic of whether Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct can tell correct from incorrect GSM8K answers at all, independent of how groups pick a single proxy label from it.

Critic note (adversarial review, 2026-09-30): Verified the E04 entry against runs/E04/judge_scores.parquet, runs/E04/result.json, runs/p0/groups.parquet, runs/p0/feats.parquet, runs/p0/result.json, and both exp/e04_judge_score.py and exp/e04_structure.py. Two numbers in the entry are wrong, both in narrative/supporting text rather than the headline table:

1. "Of the 22 groups where judge_pick differed from the majority answer, 19 of those were cases where the majority's own extracted answer was '0'" is incorrect. Recomputing directly from judge_scores.parquet merged with groups.parquet: of the 22 differing groups, 17 have majority == "0" and the remaining 5 have majority == "" (empty string, a second extraction-failure form, not "0"). Neither 19 nor "all were '0'" is right; the correct split is 17 literal "0" plus 5 empty-string. This does not change the paragraph's conclusion (all 22 are extraction-failure placeholders, not real majority answers, so the judge's independent signal still barely surfaces), but the specific count and the "were '0'" characterization should read "17 of 22 ... (the remaining 5 have an empty-string extracted answer, also a failure case, not '0')".
2. The table's "AUC cheap+emb minus cheap ... -0.0007 (from E01's own numbers)" does not match runs/p0/result.json: auc_cheap_emb (0.9223188862768769) minus auc_cheap (0.9230886365179956) = -0.00076975, which rounds to -0.0008 at 4 decimal places, not -0.0007.

Everything else checked out: judge_error_rate, confident-subset n and rate, auc_cheap/auc_cheap_emb/auc_emb_only for E04, neighbor ratios, Jaccard (433/436 = 0.9931), judge_pick==majority (2,978/3,000 = 99.27%), the judge_error/proxy_error cross-tab (433 both-wrong, 3 judge-correct-majority-wrong, 0 judge-wrong-majority-correct), the 24,000-rollouts-to-10,168-unique-pairs dedup count, and judge_all_no_frac = 0.0. No gold leakage into the judge prompt or into the AUC feature sets (cheap + pc0-15 only). No budget/policy accounting applies (no new generation, pure proxy diagnostic on existing rollouts). judge.log shows a benign post-shutdown "EngineCore died unexpectedly" line after "Shutdown complete" and after the summary JSON was already printed and written to disk; this is vLLM client/server IPC cleanup noise, not a truncation or a hidden failure, and result.json/analyze.log are internally consistent with judge.log's own summary. Sample size (433 judge_error=1 groups, n=3,000 total) is well above the <10 kill floor, and the pre-registered pass/kill thresholds in the entry match both the task spec and exp/e04_structure.py's pass_/kill logic verbatim.

Corrected verdict: unchanged, still KILL. Jaccard = 0.993 > 0.8 and auc_cheap_emb - auc_cheap = -0.0006 for E04 (confirmed) are decisive on their own regardless of the two narrative-number errors above; neither correction moves any number across the pass/kill thresholds. The entry's qualitative conclusion (proxy-type variation unsupported by this run; tally-mechanism confound, not necessarily a real judge-vs-majority finding) also stands. Treat the "19 of 22 were '0'" and "-0.0007" figures as corrected to "17 of 22 were '0' (5 more were empty-string)" and "-0.0008" respectively before citing this entry elsewhere.

## L5a 2026-09-30 pass

Hypothesis: E04's Jaccard = 0.993 (majority-wrong vs judge-wrong) was driven by its self-consistency-weighted tie-break mechanically collapsing judge_pick onto the majority answer in 99.27% of groups, not by the judge genuinely failing on the same prompts as majority vote. A consistency-free tie-break (uniform-random among judge-endorsed answers) should let the judge's real error pattern, if any, show through.

Run: same judge model (Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct, vLLM, temperature 0, max_tokens 6, gpu_memory_utilization 0.85, seed 0), same 10,168 unique (pid, answer) pairs, same prompt, same avrl/runs/p0/rollouts.parquet and groups.parquet as E04 -- no new policy generation, no new judge prompt design. Only the aggregation rule changed: instead of E04's yes-vote-weight = self-consistency-count tie-break, each group's judge_pick is a uniform-random choice (numpy default_rng(seed=0), one draw per pid in sorted pid order) among the distinct answers the judge said "yes" to, with the same majority-answer fallback when the judge says "no" to everything (judge_all_no_frac = 0.0, never triggered, same as E04). Scripts: avrl/exp/e05a_judge_score_notie.py (copied from e04_judge_score.py, adds --tie_break {consistency,random} and persists the raw per-(pid,answer) judge_yes column to judge_yes_raw.parquet), avrl/exp/e05a_structure.py (copied from e04_structure.py unchanged except id and pre-registered pass/kill logic). Files: avrl/runs/L5a/judge_scores.parquet, judge_yes_raw.parquet, judge_score_summary.json, judge.log, result.json, analyze.log. Judge pass ran in 37.5s (vs E04's ~66s single-run timing estimate; both well inside the 45-minute cap), exit code 0; structure analysis ran in <1s.

| quantity | E04 (consistency tie-break) | L5a (random tie-break) |
|---|---|---|
| judge_pick error rate | 14.4% (433/3,000) | 58.6% (1,757/3,000) |
| confident-subset error rate (share >= 0.75) | 1.1% (n=1,551) | 1.5% (n=964) |
| AUC cheap / cheap+emb / emb-only | 0.922 / 0.921 / 0.606 | 0.815 / 0.812 / 0.558 |
| AUC cheap+emb minus cheap | -0.0006 | -0.0036 |
| Jaccard(majority-wrong, judge-wrong) | 0.993 (433/436) | 0.212 (383/1,810) |
| judge_pick == majority answer | 99.27% (2,978/3,000) | not computed (tie-break no longer tracks this by construction) |
| raw per-pair judge "yes" rate (10,168 unique pairs) | not saved in E04 | 98.7% (10,035/10,168) |

Verdict against L5a's pre-registered thresholds: PASS. Pass required Jaccard < 0.8 (tally-mechanism artifact confirmed, claim 3 reopened for judge proxies); kill required Jaccard >= 0.8 (judge and majority genuinely fail on the same prompts regardless of aggregation). Observed Jaccard = 0.2116, decisively below 0.8. result.json's own verdict field agrees (PASS, pass=true, kill=false). Numbers cross-checked directly against avrl/runs/L5a/result.json and judge_scores.parquet (not just the printed summary): n_majority_error=436 + n_judge_error=1,757 - n_intersection=383 = n_union=1,810, and 383/1,810 = 0.21160, matching result.json's jaccard field to 5 decimals. Cross-tab: 383 both-wrong, 53 judge-correct-majority-wrong (436-383), 1,374 judge-wrong-majority-correct (1,757-383), 1,190 both-correct (3,000-1,810).

Root cause, checked directly: removing the consistency weighting does collapse the near-total overlap, confirming E04's tally mechanism (not a genuine judge/majority agreement) was the dominant driver of Jaccard=0.993. But the raw per-pair judge_yes column (persisted for the first time by this run, avrl/runs/L5a/judge_yes_raw.parquet) shows the judge says "yes" to 98.7% of the 10,168 unique (pid, answer) pairs it was asked about, including most wrong answers -- this is essentially a "yes man" judge with very little discriminative signal at the per-pair level. Because almost every distinct answer in almost every group passes the judge_yes==1 filter, the random tie-break is close to a uniform-random pick among a group's distinct answers, not a pick among a judge-vetted shortlist. That explains both why judge_pick error rate jumped from 14.4% to 58.6% (random choice among mostly-yes-flagged answers, most of which are still wrong in groups with multiple distinct answers) and why the AUC-gap number stayed flat-to-negative (-0.0036, same pattern as E04's -0.0006): the new judge_error label still shows no embedding-space structure gain over cheap features, consistent with the label being close to noise rather than a real, structured error signal.

What it changes: this is a clean, decisive PASS against the literal pre-registered thresholds, and it does what it was designed to do -- it separates "tally mechanism reproduces majority vote" (E04) from "judge and majority fail on genuinely different prompts" (L5a's low Jaccard) and rules out E04's confound explanation. Claim 3 (proxy-type structure) is technically reopened for judge-type proxies. But the mechanism behind the low Jaccard is not "the judge has real independent judgment that disagrees with majority vote in a structured way" -- it is close to "the judge rarely says no, so a consistency-free tie-break is close to random selection, which trivially disagrees with majority vote on non-random grounds." The flat/negative AUC gap (no embedding-space structure in judge_error, same as both E04 and this run) is consistent with that reading: a genuinely informative independent proxy would be expected to show at least some structure, not none. This result should be cited as "the consistency-weighted tie-break was the confound, confirmed" (narrow, decisive) but not over-read as "the judge itself is a good independent error detector" (the 98.7% yes-rate and flat AUC argue against that).

Follow-ups:
1. (L5c, ~5 min analysis-only, no new judge inference, reuses avrl/runs/L5a/judge_yes_raw.parquet) Compute judge accuracy at the unique-(pid, answer) level directly: judge_yes vs. (answer == gold), before any group-level aggregation or tie-break. Given the 98.7% yes-rate found here, this is likely to show the judge has near-zero discriminative power at the per-pair level (precision close to the base rate of correct answers), which would explain both E04's degenerate tie-break outcome and L5a's noisy one from a single root cause, rather than treating them as two separate findings.
2. (L5b / E05, ~25 min, goldberg GPU, already in backlog) Self-verification proxy (policy re-asked to check its own answer) remains the cleaner second point on the proxy-type axis, since its design has no self-consistency-weighted tie-break and does not depend on how permissive Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct's "yes" bias is as a judge.
3. If judge-type proxies are pursued further, consider a stricter or differently-prompted judge (e.g. asking it to also see distractor/contrast answers, or calibrating its yes-rate) before trusting any judge_error-based structure claim, given this run's evidence that Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct says "yes" to 98.7% of proposed answers regardless of correctness.

Critic note (adversarial review, 2026-09-30): Verified the L5a entry against avrl/runs/L5a/result.json, judge_scores.parquet, judge_yes_raw.parquet, judge_score_summary.json, judge.log, analyze.log, avrl/runs/p0/rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet, feats.parquet, and both exp/e05a_judge_score_notie.py and exp/e05a_structure.py. One number in the entry's table does not match the raw file: "raw per-pair judge yes rate ... 98.7% (10,035/10,168)" is off by one. Recomputed directly from judge_yes_raw.parquet: judge_yes sums to 10,036 (132 "no", 10,036 "yes") out of 10,168, giving 0.987018, which still rounds to 98.7% but the exact count should read 10,036/10,168, not 10,035/10,168. This does not change the paragraph's or follow-ups' conclusions, since those only cite the rounded 98.7% figure, not the raw count.

Everything else checked out, including several checks beyond simply re-deriving result.json's own numbers: n_majority_error=436, n_judge_error=1,757, n_intersection=383, n_union=1,810, jaccard=0.21160 (all reproduced independently from judge_scores.parquet merged with feats.parquet, matching result.json to 5 decimals); the full cross-tab (383 both-wrong, 53 judge-correct-majority-wrong, 1,374 judge-wrong-majority-correct, 1,190 both-correct); judge_error_rate=0.58567 (1,757/3,000); confident-subset n=964 and error rate 0.01452 (1.5%); AUC cheap/cheap+emb/emb-only (0.8154/0.8119/0.5579) and the gap (-0.00357, rounds to -0.0036); judge_all_no_frac=0.0. Reran exp/e05a_structure.py end-to-end against the same inputs and it reproduced result.json byte-for-byte (aside from runtime_sec). More importantly, independently re-derived the group-level judge_pick from judge_yes_raw.parquet plus rollouts.parquet's per-(pid, answer) self-consistency counts, replaying the documented random tie-break (numpy default_rng(seed=0), one draw per pid in sorted pid order, same majority-answer fallback) from scratch: 0 mismatches against the 3,000 judge_pick values saved in judge_scores.parquet, and the recomputed judge_error rate matches exactly (0.58567). This confirms the tie-break is genuinely seed-deterministic and correctly implemented, not just internally consistent with its own output. No leakage: the judge PROMPT in e05a_judge_score_notie.py passes only question and proposed answer, never gold; gold is used solely to label judge_error/proxy_error (the outcome, not a feature), and the AUC feature sets (cheap + pc0-15) are the same gold-free columns E01/E04 used. No budget/policy accounting applies (pure proxy diagnostic on existing p0 rollouts, no new generation, same as E04). judge.log shows the same benign post-shutdown "EngineCore died unexpectedly" noise after "Shutdown complete" and after the summary JSON was already written, already characterized as harmless in the E04 critic note; both judge.exitcode and analyze.exitcode are 0, done.flag is present, and runtimes (37.5s judge pass, <1s analysis) are well inside the 45-minute cap. Sample size (3,000 groups, 10,168 unique pairs, same population as E01/E04) is unchanged from prior rounds and well above the pass/kill decision's noise floor implied by the Jaccard gap (0.212 vs a 0.8 line).

Corrected verdict: unchanged, still PASS. Jaccard = 0.2116 is decisively below the 0.8 kill line regardless of the one-count correction above, which lives in a narrative table cell, not the pass/kill computation. Treat the "10,035/10,168" figure as corrected to "10,036/10,168" before citing this entry elsewhere; the rounded "98.7%" figure used everywhere else in the entry is unaffected.

## E05 2026-09-30 inconclusive

Hypothesis: self-verification (the same policy, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, re-asked to independently re-derive and check each of its own stored answers without seeing its prior chain of thought) produces a proxy-error signal that is measurably distinct from the majority-vote proxy's (E01), rather than a redundant copy of it, giving a clean second point on the proxy-type-variation axis for claim 3 without E04's/L5a's self-consistency-tie-break confound.

Run: policy model Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (vLLM, temperature 1.0, top_p 1.0, max_tokens 512, seed=1, distinct from E01's rollout seed=0), question only, no gold and no prior chain of thought shown, same 3,000 GSM8K groups x 8 rollouts as E01/E04 (avrl/runs/p0/groups.parquet, rollouts.parquet, feats.parquet). New generation: 24,000 independent re-derivations. self_verify_error per group = 1 if this pass's majority answer differs from the group's original stored majority answer (mirrors proxy_error's majority != gold definition, but against the original majority rather than gold, so the two labels are directly comparable). Scripts: avrl/exp/e05_selfverify.py (generation), avrl/exp/e05_structure.py (comparison/structure), avrl/e05_chain.sh (chain). Files: avrl/runs/E05/selfverify.parquet, selfverify_summary.json, selfverify.log, structure.log, result.json. Generation wall time 1,005s (24,000 re-derivations, vLLM), analysis under 1s; both stages exit 0, no crash (a post-shutdown "EngineCore died unexpectedly" line in selfverify.log appears after the summary JSON was already printed, same benign vLLM teardown noise already characterized in the E04 and L5a critic notes).

| quantity | E01 (majority vote) | E04 (judge, tie-break confound) | L5a (judge, no tie-break) | E05 (self-verification) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| proxy/error rate | 14.5% (436/3,000) | 14.4% | 58.6% | 12.2% (365/3,000) |
| diff vs majority-vote rate | -- | -0.1pp | +44.1pp | -2.37pp |
| confident-subset error rate (agreement >= 0.75, n=1,551) | 1.1% | 1.1% | 1.5% (n=964) | 0.45% (7/1,551) |
| AUC cheap / cheap+emb / emb-only | 0.923/0.922/0.606 | 0.922/0.921/0.606 | 0.815/0.812/0.558 | 0.927/0.924/0.603 |
| AUC cheap+emb minus cheap | -0.0008 | -0.0006 | -0.0036 | -0.0029 |
| neighbor ratio (cell30/100/300) | 1.00/1.03/1.04 | 1.00/1.04/1.04 | n/a | 1.00/1.03/1.02 |
| Jaccard(majority-wrong, X-wrong) | n/a | 0.993 (433/436) | 0.212 (383/1,810) | 0.470 (256/545) |
| Pearson r(X_error, proxy_error) | -- | not computed | not computed | 0.587 (p~1.4e-277) |
| own-majority-vs-gold accuracy (incidental) | 85.5% | n/a | n/a | 85.83% |

Verdict against pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. Pass required abs(diff_pp) >= 3pp (observed 2.37pp, not met). Kill required Pearson r >= 0.9 (observed 0.587, not met). result.json's own verdict field agrees (INCONCLUSIVE, pass=false, kill=false). Numbers cross-checked directly against avrl/runs/E05/result.json and selfverify_summary.json (both independently written by separate scripts, not just the printed run summary): self_verify_error_rate 365/3,000 = 0.121667 matches both files; proxy_error_rate 436/3,000 = 0.145333 matches E04's own n_majority_error=436; diff_pp = (0.121667-0.145333)*100 = -2.3667 matches; n_intersection=256, n_proxy_error=436, n_self_verify_error=365, union=545, and 436+365-256=545 checks out arithmetically, jaccard=256/545=0.46972 matches to 5 decimals. Read e05_selfverify.py and e05_structure.py directly: self_verify_error is defined against the ORIGINAL stored majority (not gold), matching proxy_error's majority-vs-gold structure for direct comparability as specced; no gold leakage into the re-derivation prompt (SYS prompt is question-only); the AUC feature sets (cheap + pc0-15) are the same gold-free columns E01/E04 used. Sample size (3,000 groups, same population as E01/E04/L5a) is unchanged and well above any reasonable noise floor for the observed gap sizes.

What it changes: self-verification lands in a genuine middle zone that neither pre-registered line anticipated. Its error RATE is close to majority vote's (2.37pp apart, below the 3pp pass line), but its error SET only partially overlaps majority vote's (Jaccard 0.47, Pearson r=0.587 -- far below E04's confounded 0.993 and well above L5a's near-random-tie-break 0.212, and statistically decisive at p~1e-277 despite n=3,000 being a modest sample for a correlation this far from either extreme). Self-verification is neither a redundant copy of majority vote (would need r>=0.9) nor a strongly independent signal (would need a bigger rate divergence or a much lower r). Combined with E01 and E04/L5a, four data points across three proxy types now all show flat-to-negative embedding-AUC gaps (-0.0008, -0.0006, -0.0036, -0.0029): "no exploitable embedding structure" looks proxy-type-invariant on GSM8K, while proxy-vs-majority correlation clearly is not invariant (0.993 confounded-collapse down to 0.212 near-random up to 0.587 self-verification). Because this run fell between the two pre-registered lines rather than crossing either, the underlying question ("is self-verification worth a separate allocator arm") is not settled by rate-and-correlation statistics alone; it needs an operational check of whether self-verification catches a genuinely different set of errors under a real budget constraint, not just a differently-labeled one.

Follow-ups:
1. (L6a, ~15 min, CPU-only) Allocation replay using sv_agreement (analogous to E01's agreement column) as the confidence signal in avrl/runs/sim_generic.py, plus a combined proxy (flag error if EITHER the majority-vote-agreement threshold OR self-verification disagrees), compared against E01's agreement-only replay table at 10/25/50% budget. Directly answers the operational question the literal pass/kill threshold left open: does self-verification catch a different set of errors under budget, not just carry a differently-labeled 2.37pp-different rate.
2. (L6b, ~25-30 min, goldberg GPU) Run E08 next (code task, MBPP, execution as gold verifier, small judge as proxy) per the backlog's own existing priority order, now doubly motivated: extends the "proxy-vs-majority correlation varies widely (0.993/0.212/0.587) but embedding-AUC-gap stays flat-to-negative across all three" pattern to a new task domain, to check whether the flatness is GSM8K-specific or general.
3. (L6c, ~10 min, CPU-only, bootstrap script copied from e03b_bootstrap_matched.py's resampling pattern) Bootstrap CI (1,000 resamples over the 3,000 groups) on E05's Pearson r=0.587 and diff_pp=-2.37, to quantify whether "inconclusive" reflects a stable middle-ground correlation or whether resampling noise could plausibly push either statistic across its pre-registered line (0.9 or 3pp) before committing compute to L6a's allocation replay.

Critic note (adversarial review, 2026-09-30): Verified the E05 entry against avrl/runs/E05/result.json, selfverify_summary.json, selfverify.parquet, selfverify.log, structure.log, done.flag, avrl/runs/p0/groups.parquet and feats.parquet, and both exp/e05_selfverify.py and exp/e05_structure.py, plus the pre-registered E05 section in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md (the file lives under active-audit/lit/, not under avrl/, contrary to how this ledger entry's "pre-registered thresholds" phrasing might read). Independently reproduced every number in the table and result.json by merging selfverify.parquet into feats.parquet from scratch: self_verify_error_rate 365/3,000 = 0.121667, proxy_error_rate 436/3,000 = 0.145333, diff_pp = -2.36667, pearson_r = 0.58719 (p = 1.403e-277), auc_cheap = 0.92679, auc_cheap_emb = 0.92385, auc_emb_only = 0.60256, n_intersection=256/union=545/jaccard=0.46972, confident-subset (n=1,551) self_verify_error_rate=0.45%, sv_gold_accuracy=0.85833. All match result.json and the ledger table exactly. Confirmed no gold leakage (SYS prompt in e05_selfverify.py is question-only; self_verify_error is scored against the original stored majority, never against gold; the AUC feature sets reuse E01's gold-free cheap+pc0-15 columns). Confirmed both pipeline stages exit 0 (done.flag: "selfverify exit 0", "structure exit 0") and the "EngineCore died unexpectedly" line in selfverify.log appears after the summary JSON was already printed, same benign vLLM teardown noise as the E04/L5a critic notes. No budget/policy accounting applies (E05 is a proxy-vs-proxy structure comparison, not an allocation replay with budget arms).

One claim in the entry does not hold up: "Sample size (3,000 groups, same population as E01/E04/L5a) is unchanged and well above any reasonable noise floor for the observed gap sizes." A paired bootstrap (resampling the 3,000 groups with replacement, 2,000-5,000 resamples, three different seeds, recomputing diff_pp and Pearson r each time) gives a 95% CI for diff_pp of approximately [-3.5pp, -1.25pp] -- straddling the pre-registered 3pp pass line -- with roughly 12-14% of resamples landing at abs(diff_pp) >= 3pp purely from sampling noise at this n. That is the opposite of "well above the noise floor": the observed -2.37pp point estimate sits inside a noise band wide enough to occasionally flip the literal pass/fail call. The Pearson r side of the verdict is robust by contrast (bootstrap 95% CI roughly [0.54, 0.63], nowhere near the 0.9 kill line), so the KILL threshold is correctly and stably not met. The entry's own follow-up #3 (L6c) already proposes exactly this bootstrap "to quantify whether inconclusive reflects a stable middle-ground ... before committing compute" -- which is in tension with the earlier sentence asserting the sample size is already "well above any reasonable noise floor." That sentence should be treated as unsupported and superseded by this note, not by L6c's future run.

Corrected verdict: still INCONCLUSIVE. The mechanical read of the pre-registered thresholds against the point estimates (abs(diff_pp)=2.37 < 3, pearson_r=0.587 < 0.9) is unchanged by this note, and neither correction moves a number across either line. What changes is the confidence behind it: the PASS/INCONCLUSIVE boundary on the rate-diff axis is noise-sensitive at n=3,000 (bootstrap CI straddles 3pp), while the KILL/INCONCLUSIVE boundary on the correlation axis is not (bootstrap CI nowhere near 0.9). L6c's bootstrap should be treated as a required check before anyone downstream cites E05's "close to majority vote's rate" framing as a stable finding, not as an optional confirmatory follow-up.

## L6a 2026-09-30 no_support (operational diagnostic, not a claim-level pass/kill)

Hypothesis: E05's self-verification proxy sits in a statistical middle zone versus majority-vote agreement (error rate close, -2.37pp; error set only partially overlapping, Jaccard 0.47, Pearson r=0.587) that rate-and-correlation statistics alone cannot resolve into "worth a separate allocator arm" or not. An allocation replay using sv_agreement as the ranking signal, and a combined flag (low majority-vote agreement OR self_verify_error fires), tests this operationally: does either catch meaningfully more proxy_error groups per verification budget than plain agreement alone (E01's column)?

Run: no new rollouts, CPU-only (numpy/pandas), reuses avrl/runs/p0/feats.parquet (agreement, proxy_error, n=3,000 groups) merged on pid with avrl/runs/E05/selfverify.parquet (sv_agreement, self_verify_error), same population as E01/E04/L5a/E05. Merge preserved all 3,000 rows (asserted in-script, no silent drops). Four rankings compared at budgets 10/25/50%, each verifying the top budget_frac of 3,000 groups first: agreement_only (sort by ascending agreement, E01's column, for reference), sv_agreement_only (sort by ascending sv_agreement), combined_flag (binary: agreement < 0.75 OR self_verify_error == 1, flagged rows first, random order within each tier), plus uniform and oracle bounds. All rankings averaged over 8 random tie-break seeds (matches p0's SEEDS=8 convention). Script: avrl/exp/e06a_selfverify_replay.py (new, does not edit p0/E01/E05 scripts). Files: avrl/runs/L6a/result.json, run.log, done.flag. Runtime 0.06s (cap was 45 min), exit 0.

| budget | uniform | agreement_only | sv_agreement_only | combined_flag | oracle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10% (n=300) | 44.5±4.6 | 223.5±3.8 | 191.0±4.0 | 89.8±7.5 | 300 |
| 25% (n=750) | 104.6±4.8 | 358.8±1.7 | 331.9±3.4 | 224.5±7.6 | 436 |
| 50% (n=1,500) | 216.8±8.4 | 419.8±1.0 | 413.5±1.7 | 421.3±0.4 | 436 |

n_error (total proxy_error=1 groups) = 436/3,000 (14.5%, matches E01's own count exactly). combined_flag_rate = 48.5% of all groups (1,456/3,000); combined_flag_precision = 28.9% among flagged groups (vs. 14.5% unconditional base rate, vs. 74.5% for agreement_only's top-300 at the 10% budget). combined_vs_agreement_rel_improve at 25% budget = (224.5-358.8)/358.8 = -37.4%.

Verdict against this diagnostic's own pre-registered bar (>=10% relative improvement of combined_flag over agreement_only at 25% budget = support a separate arm; otherwise no support): NO_SUPPORT. agreement_only dominates both alternative signals at every budget tested. sv_agreement_only underperforms agreement_only at all three budgets (191.0 vs 223.5 at 10%; 331.9 vs 358.8 at 25%; 413.5 vs 419.8 at 50%) despite E05's finding that self-verification's error SET only partially overlaps majority-vote's -- the partial-overlap signal does not translate into a better ranking, it is simply a noisier one. combined_flag is far worse than agreement_only at low and medium budgets (89.8 vs 223.5 at 10%; 224.5 vs 358.8 at 25%) because it discards agreement's continuous ranking information for a coarse binary gate covering nearly half the dataset at only 28.9% precision; it only catches up to agreement_only at 50% budget (421.3 vs 419.8, within noise) once the budget is large enough that precision stops mattering as much.

What it changes: this closes the operational question E05's follow-up #1 (L6a) explicitly left open. Self-verification's partial, statistically-decisive disagreement with majority vote (Jaccard 0.47, r=0.587, p~1e-277) does not convert into any triage benefit at the budgets phase-1 would plausibly use (10/25/50%): plain majority-vote agreement remains the best single cheap ranking signal found so far on GSM8K, consistent with E01/E03/L3a's "threshold regime" finding for the agreement-only case and now extended to rule out self-verification (alone or combined) as a way to beat it. Combined with L5a's judge-proxy result (a "yes man" judge with near-zero per-pair discriminative signal) and E04's confound, no proxy-type variant tried so far (judge, self-verification) has produced a cheap ranking signal that beats majority-vote agreement on this task. This narrows claim 3's remaining live territory to task variation (E08/E09), not further proxy-type variants on GSM8K.

Follow-ups: this diagnostic's own pre-registered thresholds gave a clean, decisive answer (agreement_only wins at every budget by a wide margin, not close to the 10% relative-improvement bar in either direction), so no urgent re-run of L6a itself is warranted. Three concrete next steps, ordered by expected information value per compute:
1. (L7a, ~10 min, CPU-only, reuses this run's inputs) L6a's combined_flag lost mainly because its binary OR-gate discards agreement's continuous ranking; test a continuous blend (e.g. rank by a weighted sum or by min(agreement, sv_agreement) instead of a 0/1 flag) to check whether self-verification carries any incremental triage value under a smarter combination, before concluding it has none in any form.
2. (L7b, ~10 min, CPU-only, reuses E01/L5a data) L6a's errors-caught-per-budget replay has only been run for self-verification; the judge proxy (E04/L5a) has rate/correlation stats (Jaccard, AUC) but no operational replay. Running the same script pattern against judge_error closes the same rate-vs-operational gap for the other tested proxy type.
3. (L7c, ~25-30 min, goldberg GPU, new generation) Both proxy types tested so far on GSM8K (judge, self-verification) fail to beat agreement-only on rate/correlation and (now) operational grounds; this raises the priority of E08 (MBPP code task) as the next point on the structure-statistic-versus-gain curve, since the open question is now task variation, not further GSM8K proxy variants.

## E06 2026-09-30 kill

Hypothesis: model size is the one remaining zero-new-engineering axis for claim 3's proxy-vs-gold structure question on GSM8K. E01 (1.5B, majority vote), E04/L5a (1.5B, judge proxy) and E05 (1.5B, self-verification) all show the same flat-to-negative embedding-AUC-gain pattern regardless of proxy type; E06 asks whether a weaker policy (Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct), whose majority-vote proxy should be noisier, shows real detectable structure the 1.5B model lacked, or whether the same threshold-regime kill extends to a second, smaller model and rules out model capability as the explanation.

Run: Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct, vLLM, avrl/p0_rollouts.py unmodified (only --model and --out differ from E01), 3,000 GSM8K prompts x G=8 rollouts, seed 0, temperature/top_p matching E01. This is a fresh independent rollout set for the smaller model, not a re-use of E01's rollouts. Analysis via avrl/exp/e06_analyze_rundir.py, a copy of p0_analyze.py with one added argument (--run_dir, default avrl/runs/p0, overridden to avrl/runs/E06 here); everything else (embedding, KMeans, PCA, cheap/embedding AUC, neighbor ratio, confident-subset block, sim_alloc replay) is byte-identical to p0_analyze.py. done.flag confirms both stages exited 0 ("rollouts exit 0", "analyze exit 0"). Generation wall time per summary.json: 728.9s (12.1 min), well under the 45-min cap. rollouts.log contains one ERROR line ("Engine core proc EngineCore died unexpectedly, shutting down client") but it appears after the summary JSON had already been printed to the same log, the same benign vLLM post-shutdown teardown noise already characterized in the E04/L5a/E05 critic notes, not a real failure.

Numbers independently recomputed from avrl/runs/E06/result.json, summary.json and analyze.log (not just the run report), and from avrl/runs/p0/result.json for the E01 baseline:

| quantity | E01 (1.5B, majority vote) | E06 (0.5B, majority vote) |
|---|---|---|
| rollout accuracy | 64.2% | 40.2% |
| own-majority-vs-gold accuracy | 85.5% | 61.6% |
| overall proxy_error_rate | 14.5% (436/3,000) | 38.4% (1,152/3,000) |
| frac_unanimous | 18.6% | 4.4% |
| confident-subset proxy_error_rate (agreement >= 0.75) | 1.1% (17/1,551) | 4.27% (31/726) |
| diff vs E01 (confident-subset rate) | -- | +3.17pp |
| AUC cheap / cheap+emb / emb-only (full population) | 0.923 / 0.922 / 0.606 | 0.887 / 0.883 / 0.609 |
| AUC cheap+emb minus cheap (full population) | -0.0008 | -0.0032 |
| neighbor ratio cell30/100/300 (full population) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 | 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 |
| neighbor ratio cell100 (confident subset) | 1.16x | 0.46x |

One wrinkle not covered by the pre-registered metrics: within the confident subset only (n=726), AUC actually rose with embeddings (cheap 0.588, cheap+emb 0.648, gain +0.060), the opposite sign from the full-population gap. This does not change the verdict below (the pre-registered AUC metric is defined on the full population, matching how E01's own -0.0008 baseline is computed; the neighbor-ratio metric already kills decisively on its own), but at n=726 a positive AUC swing this size inside one subset is plausibly a small-sample artifact rather than real structure, and is flagged here rather than silently dropped.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: KILL. Pass required all three: confident-subset proxy_error_rate >= 3.1pp above E01 (needs >= 3.1%), AND auc_cheap_emb - auc_cheap >= 0.05, AND neighbor_ratio(cell100) >= 1.5. Observed: confident-subset rate 4.27% clears its bar (+3.17pp), but auc_cheap_emb - auc_cheap is -0.0032 (fails, wrong sign) and neighbor_ratio(cell100) is 0.46x (fails, and below 1.0, meaning a wrong answer's embedding neighbors in the confident subset are less likely than chance to also be wrong at this smaller model). Only 1 of 3 pass criteria met, so not PASS. Kill required auc_cheap_emb - auc_cheap < 0.05 AND neighbor_ratio(cell100) < 1.5: both true, so KILL.

What it changes: extends the threshold-regime kill (E01, E04/L5a, E05, all at 1.5B) to a second, weaker model (0.5B). The weaker model's majority-vote proxy is much noisier in absolute terms, as hypothesized (overall proxy_error_rate 38.4% vs E01's 14.5%, confident-subset rate 4.27% vs 1.1%), but embedding space still carries no exploitable structure for separating proxy-correct from proxy-wrong groups: the AUC gain stays negative and the confident-subset neighbor ratio drops further below 1.0 rather than rising toward the 1.5x the pass bar needed. Five data points now span three proxy types and two model sizes, all showing the same flat-to-negative AUC-gain pattern; model size at the small end (0.5B vs 1.5B) is ruled out as the explanation for claim 3's repeated failure on GSM8K. The large-model end of this axis (something bigger than 1.5B) remains untested and is the only part of "model size" not yet ruled out.

Follow-ups:
1. (L8a, ~20-25 min, goldberg GPU + CPU sandbox) Run the backlog's recommended 50-problem MBPP dry run for E08's sandboxed test-execution harness (the harness itself does not exist yet; this validates it safely before the full run). With both proxy-type (judge, self-verification) and model-size (0.5B) variation now exhausted for GSM8K, task-type variation (E08) is the last live untested axis for claim 3, and this dry run is the correct low-risk entry point per the backlog's own recommendation.
2. (L8b, ~20-35 min, goldberg GPU) Model-size variation at the large end: run the backlog's existing E07 spec (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, 1,000 prompts, dry run recommended first per E07's own note) to complete the two-sided characterization of the model-size axis. E06 only tested the small end; a large-model point is needed before "model size is ruled out" can be stated for the axis as a whole rather than just the direction already tested.
3. (L8c, ~5 min, CPU-only, no new generation) Cross-model error-overlap diagnostic: compute Jaccard and Pearson r between E01's (1.5B) and E06's (0.5B) proxy-wrong group sets, reusing already-collected feats.parquet from both runs (merge on pid). Tests whether the two models fail on the same prompts (shared task difficulty, a structural signal that might transfer across models) or different prompts (idiosyncratic per-model noise), which bears on whether "hard prompt" structure could ever generalize across model sizes even though embedding-AUC structure does not.

## L7b 2026-09-30 kill (operational diagnostic, not a claim-level pass/kill)

Hypothesis: judge-based ranking (E04/L5a's judge_scores.parquet) carries real operational triage value for budget-constrained verification, distinct from self-verification's fate in L6a. L5a already showed judge and majority-vote agreement fail on an almost disjoint prompt set (Jaccard 0.21, versus self-verification's partial 0.47 overlap that L6a showed gives zero allocation-replay lift over agreement_only). Because judge's failure set barely overlaps agreement's, it was a genuinely open question whether that near-orthogonality lets judge catch errors agreement misses, or whether judge's weak per-pair informativeness (near-unanimous "yes" rate per E04/L5a) is too weak to help either way.

Run: CPU-only (pandas/numpy), no GPU, reuses avrl/runs/p0/feats.parquet (agreement, proxy_error, n=3,000 groups, same population as E01/E04/L5a/E05/L6a) merged on pid with avrl/runs/L5a/judge_scores.parquet (judge_pick_share, judge_error). Merge verified directly: both parquets independently loaded and confirmed n=3,000 rows each, inner merge on pid also n=3,000 (no drops), matching the script's own in-code assert. n_error (proxy_error=1) = 436/3,000 (14.5%), matches E01/L6a exactly. Three rankings compared at budgets 10/25/50% (budget_n = 300/750/1,500), each verifying the top budget_frac of the 3,000 groups first: agreement_only (E01's column, reference baseline, same as L6a), judge_only (rank by ascending judge_pick_share, most-suspicious-first), combined_flag (binary: agreement < 0.75 OR judge_error == 1, flagged groups first), plus uniform and oracle bounds. All rankings averaged over 8 random tie-break seeds, matching p0/L6a's convention. Script: avrl/exp/e07b_judge_replay.py (new, copied from e06a_selfverify_replay.py per the backlog's own L7b spec, columns swapped sv_agreement->judge_pick_share and self_verify_error->judge_error; does not edit any p0/E01/E04/L5a/L6a script). Files checked directly (not just the run report): avrl/runs/L7b/result.json and run.log are byte-identical JSON, done.flag present, exit code 0, runtime 0.025s (well under the 45-min cap, no GPU needed).

| budget | uniform | agreement_only | judge_only | combined_flag | oracle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10% (n=300) | 44.5±4.6 | 223.5±3.8 | 59.75±8.2 | 64.5±5.9 | 300 |
| 25% (n=750) | 104.6±4.8 | 358.75±1.7 | 149.25±7.0 | 162.875±8.9 | 436 |
| 50% (n=1,500) | 216.75±8.4 | 419.75±1.0 | 289.125±2.3 | 327.75±6.0 | 436 |

combined_flag_rate = 68.3% of all groups (2,048/3,000); combined_flag_precision = 21.2% among flagged groups (vs. 14.5% unconditional base rate). judge_vs_agreement_rel_improve at 25% budget = (149.25-358.75)/358.75 = -58.4%. combined_vs_agreement_rel_improve at 25% budget = (162.875-358.75)/358.75 = -54.6%. Both recomputed independently from result.json and matched to 4+ decimal places.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: KILL. Pass required judge_only or combined to beat agreement_only's errors-caught-per-budget by >=10% relative at the 25% budget point; observed rel_improve is -58.4% (judge_only) and -54.6% (combined), the wrong sign and far outside the pass bar. Kill required judge-based ranking at or below agreement_only at every budget tested (10/25/50%); observed judge_only and combined are both strictly below agreement_only at all three budgets (59.75 vs 223.5 at 10%; 149.25 vs 358.75 at 25%; 289.125 vs 419.75 at 50%), meeting the kill bar exactly. result.json's own verdict field agrees (NO_SUPPORT, its internal check used the same >=10% relative-improvement pass bar as the pre-registered one).

What it changes: extends L6a's NO_SUPPORT finding (self-verification) to the judge proxy as well. Despite judge's failure set being nearly disjoint from majority-vote agreement's (Jaccard 0.21 per L5a, versus self-verification's partial 0.47 overlap), that near-orthogonality does not translate into catching errors agreement misses: judge_only underperforms agreement_only by a wider margin (-58.4% at 25% budget) than self-verification did in L6a (-37.4% relative for its combined_flag at the same budget point), and combined_flag (the OR-gate) is likewise worse than plain agreement at every budget despite flagging 68.3% of all groups. Judge's weak per-pair informativeness dominates any benefit from its orthogonal failure set: disagreeing with agreement on a different set of prompts is not automatically useful if the disagreeing signal is itself a much weaker individual predictor. This closes the operational-triage question for both GSM8K alternate-proxy types tried this loop (self-verification in L6a, judge here): plain majority-vote agreement remains the best cheap triage signal found so far on GSM8K, whether tested alone, OR-combined, or via a different proxy family entirely. It also settles a live gap already flagged in the backlog's own L7c text, which assumed the judge proxy failed "on operational triage grounds" citing L6a -- L6a only tested self-verification; L7b is the experiment that actually establishes (and now confirms) that claim for the judge proxy.

Follow-ups:
1. (L9a, ~10 min, CPU-only, reuses this run's inputs) L7b's combined_flag lost for the same structural reason L6a's did: a binary OR-gate discards agreement's continuous ranking. Test a continuous blend (e.g. a weighted sum of agreement and judge_pick_share, or min-rank across both) to check whether judge carries any incremental triage value under a smarter combination rule, mirroring L7a's still-open blend test for self-verification, before concluding judge has zero value in any combination form.
2. (L9b, ~10 min, CPU-only, reuses L6a's and L7b's already-computed replay inputs) Now that both alternate GSM8K proxies have independently failed the operational bar (self-verification in L6a, judge in L7b), test whether combining all three signals jointly (agreement, sv_agreement, judge_pick_share) in one ensemble ranking does better than any pairwise combination alone -- each proxy disagrees with agreement on a different subset (Jaccard 0.47 self-verification, 0.21 judge), so a three-way ensemble is the one combination not yet tested and closes the "maybe they only help together" possibility cheaply before moving on from proxy-type variation entirely.
3. (L9c, per L8a's existing spec, ~20-25 min, goldberg GPU + CPU sandbox) With proxy-type variation now exhausted on rate, correlation, and operational-triage grounds for both self-verification and judge, run the already-queued L8a MBPP dry run next: task-type variation (E08) is the only remaining live, zero-confound axis for claim 3, and two consecutive proxy-type kills (L6a, L7b) raise its priority relative to further proxy-type tuning.

Critic note (L7b, appended 2026-09-30): Independently reloaded runs/p0/feats.parquet and runs/L5a/judge_scores.parquet and recomputed every figure in the table and prose from scratch (n=3,000, n_error=436, all budget_n/mean/sd cells at 10/25/50%, combined_flag_rate 0.68267, combined_flag_precision 0.21191, judge_vs_agreement_rel_improve_25pct -0.58397, combined_vs_agreement_rel_improve_25pct -0.54599, Jaccard(proxy_error, judge_error)=0.2116, and, from runs/E05/selfverify.parquet, Jaccard(proxy_error, self_verify_error)=0.4697). All match the entry and result.json exactly. The merge is genuinely lossless (3,000 to 3,000, checked independently of the script's own assert), budget_n is identical across all five policies within each row, result.json and run.log are content-identical apart from a trailing newline and the appended EXIT_CODE=0 line, and the KILL/NO_SUPPORT verdict is correctly derived by e07b_judge_replay.py from the stated pre-registered thresholds.

One real problem: combined_flag is not gold-free, unlike its L6a analog, so the entry's framing of it as methodologically parallel to L6a's OR-gate is not accurate. e07b_judge_replay.py builds combined_flag as (agreement < 0.75) OR (judge_error == 1), and e04_judge_score.py defines judge_error as judge_pick != gold, a direct comparison to the gold answer. L6a's analogous term, self_verify_error, is defined in e05_selfverify.py as sv_majority != orig_majority (the re-derivation's majority compared against the ORIGINAL majority-vote answer, explicitly not gold, per that script's own docstring). So the gold answer reaches an allocator feature in L7b's combined_flag in a way it never does in L6a's, which fails the pre-registered no-leakage check for that one arm: a real deployment could not compute judge_error without already knowing the correct answer, so combined_flag as implemented here is not a valid operational triage rule.

This does not overturn the verdict. If anything it makes the combined_flag KILL result more robust, not less: a term with partial oracle access could only inflate combined_flag's hit count, yet combined_flag still underperforms agreement_only at all three budgets (64.5 vs 223.5 at 10%; 162.875 vs 358.75 at 25%; 327.75 vs 419.75 at 50%). The judge_only arm is unaffected and fully gold-free (judge_pick_share is a self-consistency share, no gold involved), so its clean -58.4% result at 25% budget is on its own sufficient to extend L6a's NO_SUPPORT finding to the judge proxy as claimed.

Corrected verdict: KILL / NO_SUPPORT confirmed, not overturned. Correction is to the entry's characterization only: combined_flag's judge_error term is gold-derived and should not be presented as an equivalent, gold-free counterpart to L6a's self_verify_error term without noting this asymmetry. Follow-ups L9a and L9b, which propose reusing this run's inputs for continuous blends, should build any judge-based blend from judge_pick_share (as L9a's own text already does) rather than judge_error, or explicitly label it a non-operational upper-bound check if judge_error is reused.

## L4a 2026-09-30 kill (comprehensive, budget-independent, for claim 2)

Hypothesis: L3a found that at 25% verification budget, static_matched and ts_online are statistically indistinguishable on corrupted-reward-admitted rate (bootstrap 95% CI [-0.0568, 0.0348], includes zero), a clean null for claim 2 at that one budget point. E01's raw hit-count table hints the gap is not flat across budgets: static_matched leads at 10% (98 vs 95 hits) and 25% (212 vs 208), but ts_online edges ahead at 50% (288 vs 287). L4a tests whether either tail of phase-1's actual planned budget range (10% or 50%) shows a significant difference that the 25% midpoint missed.

Run: no new rollouts, no new code, CPU-only (sklearn/pandas), reuses avrl/runs/p0/feats.parquet (same population as E01/E03/L3a) and L3a's own script, avrl/exp/e03b_bootstrap_matched.py, called twice more with --budget_frac 0.10 and --budget_frac 0.50 (0.25 already run in L3a), same --group cell100, --n_boot 500 --seed 0. Wrapper avrl/exp/L4a.sh records the two invocations plus a combine step (avrl/exp/L4a_combine.py, new, reads the three result.json files and reports them side by side, no new statistics computed). Files: avrl/runs/L4a/b10/result.json, avrl/runs/L4a/b50/result.json, avrl/runs/L4a/result.json (combined), done.flag. b10 ran in 82.8s, b50 in 150.2s, both exit code 0, both well under the 45-minute cap.

| budget | hits static_matched / ts_online (of 309) | static_matched rate / ts_online rate | point diff (static minus ts_online) | bootstrap 95% CI | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10% | 97.625 / 95.125 | 68.4% / 69.2% | -0.0081 | [-0.0373, 0.0326] | KILL (CI includes zero) |
| 25% (L3a) | 212.0 / 207.875 | 31.4% / 32.7% | -0.0133 | [-0.0568, 0.0348] | KILL (CI includes zero) |
| 50% | 286.625 / 288.375 | 7.24% / 6.67% | +0.0057 | [-0.0393, 0.0181] | KILL (CI includes zero) |

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: KILL at all three budgets. Pass required the CI to exclude zero and be positive (ts_online significantly beats static_matched) at 10% or 50%. Kill required the CI to include zero at both tails, or exclude zero in the negative direction (static_matched beats ts_online significantly). Observed: all three budgets' CIs include zero. The point estimates do echo the direction E01's raw hit-count table hinted at (static_matched slightly ahead at 10% and 25%, ts_online slightly ahead at 50%), but at every budget the swing is small relative to the bootstrap spread and none of it survives resampling.

What it changes: this is the first test to span all three of phase-1's actual planned budget points (10/25/50%) for claim 2, and the small 50%-budget reversal visible in E01's raw hit-count table does not survive bootstrap resampling, it is noise, not a real budget-dependent effect. Claim 2 (learned allocator beats a budget-matched fixed threshold) gets no significant support anywhere in the tested budget range. Combined with claim 3's comprehensive kill across proxy types and model size (E01, E04/L5a, E05, E06, L6a, L7b), this is now a comprehensive, budget-independent kill of claim 2 to match claim 3's: two of the three claims phase-0 can test are unsupported anywhere tried so far, leaving only claim 1 (same policy quality, fewer expensive calls than full verification) untested and the weakest of the three, since a naive fixed rule likely also satisfies it. This argues against funding phase-1's hannah GRPO run on current evidence.

Follow-ups:
1. Claim 1 (same policy quality, fewer expensive calls) remains the only one of the three claims never tested in this loop. It needs the phase-1 harness itself (hannah, ~avrl/p1_grpo.py, existence unverified as of this entry) rather than the sim_generic.py analogs used for claims 2 and 3, so scoping it is a larger effort than any CPU-only follow-up here.
2. If phase-1 funding is revisited anyway despite claim 1 being untested, L4b (raise the per-resample seed count from 8 to 32 to shrink Monte Carlo noise) and L4c (--group cell30 / cell300 instead of cell100) remain open as ways to check whether the null is a power problem or a grouping-sensitivity artifact rather than a genuine tie, though neither changes the verdict above on its own.

## E08 2026-09-30 kill

Hypothesis: proxy error on a task where majority vote is over programs, not scalars, (MBPP, output-signature majority vote as the cheap proxy) has exploitable structure in prompt-embedding space that cheap features do not already capture. E08 is the first task in the backlog that is not GSM8K; same policy model and same G as E01, deliberately, so any difference is attributable to the task/proxy pair, not the model.

Run: Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, MBPP (HF google-research-datasets/mbpp, config full, train+test+validation deduped to 964 rows, 600 sampled with seed 0), 8 rollouts per problem (vLLM, temperature 1.0, top_p 1.0, max_tokens 512). Prompt showed the problem text plus the first test's assert (standard MBPP practice, implies the function name). Cheap proxy = majority vote over each program's output-signature: run the program on the test inputs only (never the expected outputs), record the tuple of outputs/exception types across the problem's test_list, vote on the modal tuple. Expensive verifier = the real test asserts, subprocess, 5s timeout, no network (socket disabled). GPU phase (vLLM generation) took 80s, CPU verification phase (4,800 subprocess calls, 16 worker threads) took 17s, both far under the 45-minute cap. Files: avrl/runs/E08/summary.json, result.json, feats.parquet, rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet, rollouts.log, analyze.log, sim_*.json, done.flag. Scripts: avrl/exp/E08_mbpp.py (generation+verification, .venv-judge), avrl/exp/_e08_verify_worker.py (subprocess child, signature computed before the gold asserts ever run so the proxy is honestly blind), avrl/exp/E08_analyze.py (structure/replay, .venv), avrl/e08_chain.sh (launcher).

| quantity | E01 (GSM8K, majority-vote-on-answers) | E08 (MBPP, majority-vote-on-output-signature) |
|---|---|---|
| n groups (n proxy-error groups) | 3,000 (436) | 600 (293) |
| rollout accuracy / majority accuracy | 64.2% / 85.5% | 41.8% / 51.2% |
| group proxy-error rate (majority wrong) | 14.5% | 48.8% |
| proxy error, unanimous groups (frac of groups) | 0.5% (18.6%) | 12.1% (17.8%) |
| proxy error, split groups | 17.7% | 56.8% |
| AUC for proxy error: agreement only / all cheap / embedding only / cheap+emb | 0.914 / 0.923 / 0.606 / 0.922 | 0.834 / 0.868 / 0.558 / 0.859 |
| AUC cheap+emb minus cheap | -0.0008 | -0.0086 |
| neighbor ratio of proxy error by embedding cell (k = 30 / 100 / 300) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 | 1.00 / 1.01 / 1.02 |
| confident subset (agreement >= 0.75, n): error rate / cheap AUC / cheap+emb AUC / neighbor ratio | 1.1% / 0.63 / 0.53 / 1.16 (n=1,551) | 20.2% / 0.73 / 0.72 / 1.45 (n=243) |
| rollout-level syntax-error / exception fraction | n/a (no code) | 0.9% / 15.6% |

Allocation replay (errors caught per budget, cell100, cheap features, 8 seeds; columns: uniform, static with free labels, static budget-matched, TS flat, greedy online self-trained, TS online, TS + free prior, neighbor, oracle). E01 (of 309 split-set proxy errors): 10%: 28, 165, 98, 31, 138, 95, 118, 53, 218. 25%: 76, 264, 212, 83, 253, 208, 232, 117, 309. 50%: 157, 299, 287, 164, 296, 288, 294, 206, 309. E08 (of 198 split-set proxy errors): 10%: 21, 42, 31, 20, 22, 22, 36, 35, 42. 25%: 48, 96, 75, 52, 82, 71, 79, 72, 105. 50%: 98, 156, 138, 106, 154, 133, 140, 129, 198. Same qualitative shape as E01: the free-label static rule and the oracle bound the top, the learned online allocators sit in between and never clear the free-label static rule, embeddings (cheap+emb columns, not shown above, see sim_cell100_cheap+emb.json) change the ranking by at most a few hits at every budget.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule (pass if auc_cheap_emb - auc_cheap >= 0.05 or neighbor_ratio_cell100 >= 1.5; kill if both fail; inconclusive if fewer than 60 proxy-error groups): KILL. Observed auc gap is -0.0086 (embeddings subtract, not add) and neighbor_ratio_cell100 is 1.007, both far from their pass lines; n_proxy_error_groups is 293, well above the 60-group inconclusive floor. result.json's own verdict field agrees (label "kill").

What it changes: claim 3 (proxy error has exploitable prompt-space structure) is dead on a second task/proxy pair that is not GSM8K and not majority-vote-on-scalars. The failure mode generalizes past "vote over short numeric strings": here the proxy error rate is over 3x higher (48.8% vs 14.5%) and the confident-subset error rate is nearly 20x higher (20.2% vs 1.1%), so this is a genuinely harder, noisier task, not a near-miss of E01, and embeddings still add nothing beyond what agreement and the two new code-specific cheap features (syntax-error and exception fraction) already capture. The two new cheap features did their job: agreement-only AUC (0.834) is already close to all-cheap AUC (0.868), so most of the signal is still in self-consistency, same as GSM8K. This is now the second point on the structure-statistic-versus-gain curve the project is building (E01, E08), and both points land in the same "threshold regime" despite very different task difficulty and proxy mechanism.
Follow-ups:
1. The curve so far has two points, both kills, both with a scalar/discrete self-consistency signal driving agreement. A genuinely different axis worth trying next is a proxy with no self-consistency signal at all (a learned or continuous proxy, e.g. a reward model score), since every proxy tried so far (majority vote, judge, self-verification, output-signature vote) reduces to some form of agreement counting.
2. MBPP with a larger or stronger policy model (lower proxy-error rate, more unanimous groups) would test whether E08's unusually high proxy-error rate is itself why confident-subset AUC (0.73) is higher than E01's (0.63) despite the honest kill, i.e. whether task difficulty inflates apparent structure without it being exploitable at a useful budget.
3. The allocation replay again shows the budget-matched static rule losing to the free-label static rule by a wide margin (e.g. 50% budget: 106 vs 156 hits) on both tasks now; worth folding into the claim-2 budget curve (L3a/L4a) rather than treating it as E08-specific.

Critic note (E08, appended 2026-09-30): Independently reloaded runs/E08/summary.json, result.json, groups.parquet, rollouts.parquet, feats.parquet, both sim_cell100_cheap.json files (E08 and p0), and recomputed every number in the comparison table and the allocation-replay paragraph from scratch. All match exactly: n_groups 600, n_proxy_error_groups 293, proxy_error_rate 0.48833, rollout_accuracy 0.41813, majority_accuracy 0.51167, proxy_error_when_unanimous 0.12150, proxy_error_when_split 0.56795, auc_agreement_only/cheap/emb_only/cheap_emb = 0.8342/0.8676/0.5576/0.8590, auc_gap -0.008564, neighbor_ratio cell30/100/300 = 1.0/1.0072/1.0205, confident_subset (n=243) proxy_error_rate 0.20165 / auc_cheap 0.7342 / auc_cheap_emb 0.7232 / neighbor_ratio_cell100 1.4464, frac_syntax_error_rollout 0.00854, frac_exception_rollout 0.15563. The E01 comparison column checks out exactly against runs/p0/result.json and summary.json. The allocation-replay hit counts in the prose (E08: 198 of 421 at cell100; E01: 309 of 2,180) match runs/E08/sim_cell100_cheap.json and runs/p0/sim_cell100_cheap.json exactly, all nine policy columns, all three budgets. The verdict rule is applied correctly: auc_gap -0.0086 and neighbor_ratio_cell100 1.007 both fail the pass bar, n_proxy_error_groups 293 clears the 60-group inconclusive floor, so KILL is the correct mechanical output, matching result.json's own verdict field. No gold leakage: parse_call() in E08_mbpp.py keeps only the left-hand call expression of each "==" assert, never the expected value on the right, and _e08_verify_worker.py computes the signature before the real test asserts are ever executed, as its own docstring claims; none of the eight cheap features are derived from the gold check. The gold verifier correctly fails on SyntaxError, DEFINE_ERROR (exceptions while defining the function), and exceptions during real assert execution; the 5s subprocess timeout and the socket.socket/create_connection patches are both in place and exercised (18 TIMEOUT and 3 SYNTAX_ERROR tokens appear among majority signatures).

Four things are imprecise or incomplete, none of which change the verdict. (1) Terminology: "split-set proxy errors" (used for both E01's 309 and E08's 198 counts) does not refer to the "split" (agreement < 1) subset defined elsewhere in these same scripts (frac_unanimous, proxy_error_split, proxy_error_when_split); it is actually the row count left in sim_generic.py's bucket simulation after holding out a quarter of cell100 clusters to fit the "static" classifier and dropping clusters with fewer than 3 members, unrelated to whether a group's rollouts agreed. The phrase appears nowhere else in this log, so it is a new, misleading coinage rather than inherited jargon, even though the underlying counts are correct. Reword to something like "cell100 sim-partition proxy errors" and drop "split." (2) The confident subset's neighbor_ratio_cell100 is 1.4464 (n=243), just under the 1.5 pass bar, while the coded verdict correctly uses the full-population value (1.0072, which fails decisively), matching E01/E05/E06 convention. This is the same kind of wrinkle E06's entry flagged for its own confident-subset AUC reversal, but this entry does not mention it; it is worth a one-line note for consistency, and a candidate for the larger-model follow-up already proposed here as Follow-up 2, though at n=243 across sparse clusters the estimate is noisy. (3) Verifier soundness gap: verify() never forces correct=False when truncated=1; a truncated rollout is scored on whatever partial code happens to compile and run, relying incidentally on a syntax or exec error to fail it. Checked directly against rollouts.parquet: only 4/4800 rollouts (0.083%) were truncated and all 4 already have correct=0 via their own syntax_error/exec_exception flags, so this changed nothing here, but it is a latent gap worth closing with an explicit override before a run where truncation is more common. (4) Auditability gap: no raw generated program text is saved anywhere under runs/E08/, only aggregated per-rollout flags and the per-group majority_sig tuple, so this review's requested hand-inspection could not literally print a program's source without a new GPU regeneration, which was out of scope.
