Inspecting 10 random groups (seed 12345) on the available columns instead: when agreement is high, pass_rate tracks agreement almost exactly and majority_correct=1 (pids 188, 475, 134, 383); when agreement is low (near-total 8-way disagreement), the majority is an arbitrary tied pick and usually wrong (pids 234, 413, 404, 121, 467, 592), consistent with the reported 56.8% proxy_error_when_split, and the signature values themselves are rich and varied (ints, floats, lists, nested tuples), not a degenerate constant. This supports the vote doing what the design intends, but future code-task runs should persist at least a sample of raw program text so this check does not depend on GPU regeneration. Separately, "fraction of rollouts with no code block" could not be isolated exactly because the syntax_error flag conflates genuine compile SyntaxError with the code=None/"NO_CODE" path; the combined rate is 41/4800 = 0.85% and no group's majority signature is ever "NO_CODE" (checked directly, 0/600), so this is a safe upper bound, too small to make the headline numbers uninterpretable.

Corrected verdict: KILL confirmed, not overturned. All four points above are documentation, terminology, or latent-methodology notes; none of them move a reported number, and the pre-registered rule (auc_gap -0.0086, neighbor_ratio_cell100 1.007, n_proxy_error_groups 293) mechanically yields KILL exactly as the entry and result.json already state.

## P1-goldberg-s0 2026-10-01 kill (claim 2, one seed; claim 1 tie)
Hypothesis: inside GRPO training, a learned online allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards and reaches higher accuracy than a fixed agreement-threshold allocator at the same expensive-call budget (claims 1 and 2), and the gap holds or grows over training (pre-registered decision rule from GAP_cannot-work.md).
Run: avrl/p1_grpo.py on goldberg (RTX A4000 16GB, HF generation), Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, LoRA r=32, GSM8K train, 8 prompts x G=8 per step, 100 steps, budget 0.25 (2 of 8 groups verified per step, identical for both arms), seed 0, greedy eval on 300 fixed test prompts every 25 steps. Wall: 89 s/step, 2.5 h per arm. Files: avrl/runs/p1_threshold/, avrl/runs/p1_learned/, avrl/runs/p1_goldberg_summary.json.

| quantity | threshold (fixed rule) | learned (online allocator) |
|---|---|---|
| expensive calls / groups | 200 / 800 | 200 / 800 |
| corrupted-reward groups admitted (of 600 unverified) | 27 (4.5%) | 72 (12.0%) |
| corrupted by 25-step window | see summary json | see summary json |
| eval accuracy at steps 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 | 62.3 / 65.7 / 65.7 / 64.7 | 62.7 / 64.7 / 65.3 / 65.3 |

Verdict: kill for claim 2 at this scale (the threshold admits 2.7x fewer corrupted rewards); tie for claim 1 (final accuracy within the 300-prompt noise of about 2.7 points). The in-training result matches the replay (L3a, L4a): on GSM8K with majority vote, agreement is a near-sufficient confidence signal and the learned allocator cannot beat it. One seed only; seed 1 is running now so the decision rule (gap across seeds and across training windows) can be applied properly. Follow-ups: seeds 1 and 2; the hannah four-arm run at 150 steps adds full and random bounds; then the same pair on a task where the cheap confidence signal is weak, which no point so far provides.

## P1-goldberg-s1 2026-10-01 kill (claim 2 replicated; claim 1 tie)
Hypothesis and run: identical to P1-goldberg-s0 with --seed 1 (different prompt order and sampling). Files: avrl/runs/p1_threshold_s1/, avrl/runs/p1_learned_s1/.

| quantity | threshold s0 / s1 | learned s0 / s1 |
|---|---|---|
| expensive calls (of 800 groups) | 200 / 200 | 200 / 200 |
| corrupted-reward groups admitted (of 600 unverified) | 27 / 30 | 72 / 90 |
| corrupted per 25-step window | 14,5,2,6 / 9,6,12,3 | 17,17,14,24 / 21,23,28,18 |
| eval accuracy at step 100 (300 prompts) | 64.7 / 61.3 | 65.3 / 62.3 |

Verdict: kill for claim 2, replicated across two seeds (threshold admits 2.7x and 3.0x fewer corrupted rewards; the learned allocator's admissions do not fall over training in either seed). Tie for claim 1 (learned +0.6 and +1.0 points, inside the 300-prompt noise of about 2.7 points; a third seed would not change the picture at this effect size). The decision rule from the proposal (gap must hold or grow in the learned allocator's favour beyond seed variance) fails in the opposite direction.
What it changes: the in-training test agrees with every replay point. On a task with a self-consistency signal, the fixed agreement threshold is the right allocator and the learned one is strictly worse on corrupted-reward admission. The remaining question for the thesis is whether any task lacks such a signal (E09, relaunching after a context-length fix).

## E09 2026-10-01 kill

Hypothesis: every point so far (E01, L5a, E05, E06, E08) has a self-consistency confidence signal (agreement of sampled answers) with AUC 0.82-0.93 for proxy error, which puts them in the threshold regime. Claim 3 can only survive on a task where that signal is unavailable or weak, so E09 uses an open-ended task (HelpSteer2 prompts, free-text answers, no majority vote) with a small reward model as the cheap proxy and a 7B judge as the expensive verifier.

Run: Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct policy, 1500 HelpSteer2 prompts (nvidia/HelpSteer2, deduped to unique prompts, seed 0), 8 rollouts/prompt (vLLM, temperature 1.0, top_p 1.0, max_tokens 384). Cheap proxy = Skywork-Reward-V2-Qwen3-0.6B scalar reward score; within-group top half -> proxy=1; a separate global threshold (-2.141) gives proxy_pass positive rate 0.695 vs gold positive rate 0.696 (chosen to match). Expensive verifier = Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct (fallback_used=True), fixed 1-10 helpfulness+correctness rubric, temperature 0, max_tokens 8, gold = score>=7; judge output was unparseable (no digit in the 8-token completion) in 0.033% of rollouts, scored gold_positive=0 in that case. Group-level proxy_error = 1 if the group's top-RM response is not gold-positive. Files: avrl/runs/E09/summary.json, result.json, feats.parquet, rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet, gen/judge/score_rm/analyze logs, sim_*.json, done.flag. Scripts: avrl/exp/E09_judge_task.py (gen/judge/score_rm stages, .venv-judge then .venv), avrl/exp/E09_analyze.py (.venv), avrl/e09_chain.sh (launcher).

| quantity | E01 (GSM8K, majority vote) | E08 (MBPP, output-sig vote) | E09 (HelpSteer2, RM proxy / judge) |
|---|---|---|---|
| n groups (n proxy-error groups) | 3,000 (436) | 600 (293) | 1,500 (317) |
| group proxy-error rate | 14.5% | 48.8% | 21.1% |
| cheap-signal-only AUC (agreement / agreement / consistency) | 0.914 | 0.834 | 0.674 |
| AUC: all cheap / embedding only / cheap+emb | 0.923 / 0.606 / 0.922 | 0.868 / 0.558 / 0.859 | 0.734 / 0.680 / 0.768 |
| AUC cheap+emb minus cheap | -0.0008 | -0.0086 | 0.0346 |
| neighbor ratio by embedding cell (k=30/100/300) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 | 1.00 / 1.01 / 1.02 | 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.08 |
| confident subset (def. differs: agreement>=0.75 for E01/E08, top-quartile rm_gap12 for E09), n: error rate / cheap AUC / cheap+emb AUC / neighbor ratio | 1.1% / 0.63 / 0.53 / 1.16 (n=1,551) | 20.2% / 0.73 / 0.72 / 1.45 (n=243) | 16.0% / 0.74 / 0.78 / 0.94 (n=375) |

Verdict against the pre-registered rule (pass if auc_cheap_emb-auc_cheap>=0.05 or neighbor_ratio_cell100>=1.5; kill if both fail; inconclusive if fewer than 60 proxy-error groups): KILL. Observed auc gap is 0.0346 and neighbor_ratio_cell100 is 0.998; n_proxy_error_groups is 317. result.json's own verdict field agrees (label 'kill').

What it changes: Even with no discrete self-consistency signal available at all, proxy error (reward-model top-pick not gold-positive) still does not cluster in prompt-embedding space beyond what cheap scalar features (RM score stats, length, consistency) already capture. This is the third kill (E01, E08, E09) across three structurally different proxy mechanisms (majority vote over answers, majority vote over output signatures, reward-model score), which narrows claim 3's remaining room: no proxy mechanism tried so far gives embeddings anything to add.

Follow-ups:
1. If kill or inconclusive: the remaining untried axis for claim 3 is a stronger/weaker judge-proxy capability gap (e.g. a 1.5B proxy against this same 7B judge, or this reward model against a weaker judge) or a multi-step agentic task, rather than another single-turn task.
2. Judge parse failures under max_tokens=8 are an honest data-quality cost of this design; a targeted retry with more tokens on unparseable rows would tighten the gold labels if this point is extended or reused.
3. judge_model_used=Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct (fallback_used=True): if the 7B judge had to fall back to the 3B, the judge itself is weaker than planned and the gold labels should be treated as noisier than E01/E08's gold (exact answer / test execution).
4. The allocation replay (sim_*.json under avrl/runs/E09/) should be folded into the claim-2 budget curve (L3a/L4a) alongside E01 and E08, same as E08's entry proposed.


Critic note (adversarial review, 2026-10-01): Verified every number in the E09 entry against avrl/runs/E09/summary.json, result.json, gen.parquet, judged.parquet, rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet, feats.parquet, the gen/judge/score_rm/analyze logs, done.flag, and all four sim_*.json files, plus avrl/exp/E09_judge_task.py, avrl/exp/E09_analyze.py and avrl/e09_chain.sh. Independently reproduced every figure in the ledger table and result.json from scratch: n_groups=1,500, n_proxy_error_groups=317, proxy_error_rate=0.21133, gold_positive_rate=0.69625, rm_threshold=-2.140625, proxy_pass_positive_rate=0.69542, mean_consistency=0.69073, frac_truncated_rollout=0.3405, auc_consistency_only=0.67393, auc_cheap=0.73387, auc_cheap_emb=0.76846, auc_emb_only=0.67996, neighbor_ratio cell30/100/300=1.000/0.99831/1.08492, confident_subset n=375 error_rate=16.0% auc_cheap=0.7373 auc_cheap_emb=0.7823 neighbor_ratio_cell100=0.9427 -- all match exactly. The verdict correctly follows the pre-registered rule as coded: auc_gap=0.03459<0.05 and neighbor_ratio_cell100=0.99831<1.5, both fail, n_proxy_error_groups=317>=60, so KILL is the correct mechanical output (the differently-worded informal read in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md's E09 section agrees too: auc_cheap=0.734 is under 0.75 but neighbor_ratio_cell100 is not over 1.5, so that reading also misses "allocator regime"). No leakage: score_rm's rm_score is computed from (prompt, response) text only; the cheap feature set (rm_max/mean/std/gap12, token stats, trunc_frac, log_qlen, consistency) never touches gold_score/gold_positive; E09_analyze.py uses gold-derived proxy_error only as the y label, never as a feature; prompt embeddings are built from G.prompt only. Budget accounting is internally consistent: sim_alloc.py (MIN=3, K=1, SEEDS=8, BUDGET_FRACS=(0.10,0.25,0.50)) applies identical budget group-visit counts (25/59/117 for cell100, 23/61/130 for cell30) across all nine replay policies within each bucketing, for both the cheap and cheap+emb feature sets; cell100 vs cell30 differ only because the two bucketings start from different cluster counts before the standard 1/4-held-out-for-fit split. No crash: all five pipeline stages exit 0 per done.flag; the "EngineCore died unexpectedly" lines in gen.log and judge.log both occur after their respective summary JSON had already printed, the same benign vLLM teardown noise as the E04/L5a/E05 critic notes.

Three real issues change how much this kill should be trusted, though none overturn it. (1) Judge identity: the 7B judge did not fail for a transient reason -- it loaded its 14.25 GiB of weights fine, then CUDA-OOM'd 56 MiB short of fitting during vLLM's torch.compile/cudagraph profiling pass on the A4000's 16 GB card (judge.log), leaving well under 1 GB of headroom at gpu_memory_utilization=0.92, which is a near-certain failure on this hardware/settings combination rather than a fluke. The pre-registered E09 design note added the same day (lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md line 859) asserted the 7B judge "runs in 16 GB in bf16... at short outputs" with no dry run to confirm it, despite a same-day adjacent backlog entry (L8b) already flagging that this card is confirmed 16 GB, not the 32 GB some ground-rules doc assumed. So the verifier that actually ran is 3B against a 1.5B policy, a smaller capability gap than E09 was designed around; this is honestly surfaced in the entry's own follow-up 3, but is worth underlining as a near-certain, foreseeable failure rather than a one-off. (2) Gold-label quality: the judge's score distribution is coarse (of 12,000 scores: 37.4% are 8, 20.2% are 10, but only 0.1% are 9 -- a classic small-model round-number bias) though not degenerate (91% of groups show >=2 distinct scores, mean within-group std 1.50). Rollout-level RM-vs-judge correlation is moderate (pearson r=0.376). Spot-checking 10 random groups shows the RM is a directionally sensible but noisy proxy: it penalizes refusal-like responses on average (mean rm_score -1.62 vs -0.62 for others) and penalizes truncation (-0.85 vs -0.51 for complete responses), alongside genuine mis-rankings (e.g. pid 647 and pid 1284, where the RM's top pick scored worse on the judge than lower-ranked alternatives in the same group). Oddly, the judge itself rewards truncation on average (mean gold_score 7.49 truncated vs 6.82 complete) while the RM penalizes it -- a length/verbosity bias specific to the fallback judge that works against the RM, not evidence the RM carries no information. (3) proxy_error label composition: 95/1,500 groups (6.3%) are "all gold-negative" (every one of the 8 sampled responses scored under 7), so proxy_error=1 there is guaranteed regardless of which response any proxy, however good, would pick; these unwinnable groups are 95/317 = 30% of all counted proxy-error groups. Separately, 276/1,500 prompts (18.4%) still carry un-stripped HelpSteer2 multi-turn dialogue markup ("<extra_id_1>Assistant/User...") concatenated into the "prompt" field used for the policy, the judge rubric, and the prompt embeddings that drive the cell/neighbor-ratio structure statistics -- a real upstream data-cleaning bug in the prompt extraction, not previously flagged in this ledger; these contaminated prompts have proxy_error rate 27.2% vs 19.8% for clean prompts. To test whether either issue is driving the kill, I recomputed auc_cheap/auc_cheap_emb on the 1,159 groups left after excluding both the 95 unwinnable groups and the 276 contaminated prompts (still 177 proxy-error groups, above the 60-group floor): auc_cheap=0.7260, auc_cheap_emb=0.7696, gap=0.0436 -- still below the 0.05 pass bar and barely different from the full-dataset 0.0346.

Verdict: KILL is confirmed, not corrected. The core finding -- that RM-based cheap features plus prompt embeddings together still fail to clear the pre-registered bar for proxy_error on this open-ended task -- looks substantively real rather than an artefact of the three confounds checked here, since the robustness recompute controlling for the worst two of them (unwinnable groups, prompt contamination) reproduces essentially the same gap. What should change is confidence calibration, not the label: E09 is a noisier, lower-confidence kill than E01/E08's, because its gold signal comes from a fallback 3B judge with its own coarse-scoring and length biases rather than the intended 7B. Before citing E09 as having closed the task-variation axis for claim 3, it should be rerun with (a) the actual 7B judge on hardware/quantization that can hold weights plus KV cache plus compile overhead, confirmed with a small dry run first, and (b) a prompt loader that strips the HelpSteer2 "<extra_id_1>" markup.

## L9a 2026-10-01 pass

Hypothesis: E09's own entry (above) killed claim 3 (structure) on the first task where the cheap confidence signal is weak (consistency/cheap AUC 0.73, vs 0.83-0.91 on E01/E08). This is the budget-curve follow-up for claim 2 on the same task: does the learned online allocator (greedy_online, ts_online) catch more proxy-error groups per budget than a fair, budget-matched fixed threshold (static_matched), on the one task so far where the threshold signal itself is weak? Pre-registered rule: pass if greedy_online beats static_matched at the 25% budget with a 95% CI on the relative improvement that excludes zero and is positive; kill if the CI excludes zero on the negative side; inconclusive otherwise.

Run: no new rollouts, CPU-only (sklearn/pandas), reuses avrl/runs/E09/feats.parquet (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, HelpSteer2, reward-model proxy vs 3B-fallback judge gold, 1,500 groups, label proxy_error, same population as the E09 entry above). Script: avrl/exp/e09_bootstrap.py, copied and modified from avrl/exp/e03b_bootstrap_matched.py (adds the free-label "static" and "greedy_online" policies, both lifted verbatim from runs/sim_generic.py, and sweeps feature set and group_col instead of running one fixed combination). Policies: static (free-label classifier fit once on a held-out quarter of group_col buckets, deterministic), static_matched (budget-matched: half of the audit budget spent on random labels, fits, ranks the rest, no labels beyond budget), greedy_online and ts_online (self-trained, online-refit every R verified labels; greedy has no bucket layout, ts_online Thompson-samples buckets), K=1 group-level budgets (matches sim_alloc.py's own convention for this feats.parquet, confirmed by reading avrl/runs/E09/sim_alloc.py directly). Swept: group_col in {cell100, cell30}, feature set in {cheap (9 cols: rm_max, rm_mean, rm_std, rm_gap12, mean_tokens, std_tokens, trunc_frac, consistency, log_qlen), cheap_pc (+pc0..pc15, identical column lists to E09_analyze.py's own "cheap"/"embc")}, budget_frac in {0.10, 0.25, 0.50}, comparisons {greedy_online vs static_matched, ts_online vs static_matched, greedy_online vs static}. Within a resample/group_col/budget all four policies share the same fit_b/sim split and the same budget_n = int(budget_frac * len(sim)), so budget accounting is identical across policies. 300 bootstrap resamples of the 1,500 groups (seed 0, group-level, with replacement), 8 policy seeds per resample (matches p0/E03/L3a/E08 convention), 300 resamples used in full (no degenerate skips, no reduction to 200 needed). Runtime 1,440.5s (24.0 min), under the 30 min cap. Files: avrl/exp/e09_bootstrap.py, avrl/runs/E09/bootstrap.json. Sanity check: point estimates (resample 0) for the cheap/cell100 and cheap/cell30 configs match avrl/runs/E09/sim_cell100_cheap.json and sim_cell30_cheap.json exactly at all three budgets (e.g. cell100/cheap/25%: static 117.0, static_matched 98.625, greedy_online 111.125, ts_online 105.375, all match to the displayed decimal), confirming the reimplementation reproduces the existing, independently-computed replay before trusting the new bootstrap CIs built on top of it.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule, applied to the primary config (cell100, cheap, matches E01/E03/L3a/L4a/E08's standard reporting config): PASS. greedy_online vs static_matched at 25% budget: point diff +12.5 hits (111.1 vs 98.6), relative improvement +12.7%, 95% CI on the relative improvement [+3.9%, +25.5%], excluding zero on the positive side. This is the first config in the whole project (E01, E03, L3a, L4a, E08, P1-goldberg-s0/s1) where the learned allocator significantly beats a fair, budget-matched threshold.

What it changes: this is the first point where the cheap signal is weak enough (cheap AUC 0.73, vs 0.83-0.91 on E01/E08, and the AUC gap/neighbor-ratio structure test above still killed claim 3) that the learned allocator earns a real, bootstrap-significant edge over static_matched, exactly the regime the project's structure-statistic-versus-gain curve predicted claim 2 would need. The result generalizes qualitatively across both group_col groupings and both feature sets at 25% and 50% budget (greedy_online vs static_matched excludes zero on the positive side in 7 of 8 of those cells; the one exception, cell100/cheap_pc/25% for ts_online, still trends positive) but not at 10% budget, where every CI in the table includes zero -- the edge needs enough budget to let the online classifier refit before it beats a threshold fit once on a free-labeled quarter. ts_online is weaker and less consistent than greedy_online (fails to exclude zero at cell100/cheap_pc/25% and cell30/cheap_pc/50%): the plain self-trained ranking without Thompson layout over buckets, not the bucket-level exploration logic, is carrying this result. Caveat that keeps this from being an unqualified win for claim 2: greedy_online never significantly beats "static" (the free-label analog) at any budget or config tested here -- every greedy_online-vs-static CI in the table includes zero, and the point estimate favors static at 10% budget in 3 of 4 configs. So the learned allocator closes most but not all of the gap to a threshold that gets free labels beyond its budget; it does not yet surpass that stronger, budget-unmatched baseline. Given E09's own caveats (fallback 3B judge, 30% of proxy-error groups structurally unwinnable, 18% prompt contamination, all noted in the entry above and not reverified here), this PASS should be read as a real but noisier signal than E01/E08's clean kills, not a final word on claim 2.

Follow-ups:
1. The in-training rerun on this task (harness v2, following P1-goldberg-s0/s1's pattern but swapped onto HelpSteer2/RM-proxy/judge-gold instead of GSM8K) is the test that actually matters for claim 2: this entry and P1-goldberg-s0/s1 together show the replay and the in-training result agreeing exactly on GSM8K (both kill), so the replay's first PASS here needs the same in-training check before it changes any funding decision.
2. A second open-ended dataset (different prompt distribution and/or a different cheap-vs-expensive proxy pair, e.g. a weaker RM or a different judge-capability gap) would test whether "weak cheap signal" is the right generalizable condition for claim 2 to hold, or whether this PASS is specific to E09's particular proxy pair and its noted gold-label issues.

## E09b 2026-10-01 kill

Hypothesis: every point so far (E01, L5a, E05, E06, E08) has a self-consistency confidence signal (agreement of sampled answers) with AUC 0.82-0.93 for proxy error, which puts them in the threshold regime. Claim 3 can only survive on a task where that signal is unavailable or weak, so E09 uses an open-ended task (HelpSteer2 prompts, free-text answers, no majority vote) with a small reward model as the cheap proxy and a 7B judge as the expensive verifier.

Run: Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct policy, 1500 HelpSteer2 prompts (nvidia/HelpSteer2, deduped to unique prompts, seed 0), 8 rollouts/prompt (vLLM, temperature 1.0, top_p 1.0, max_tokens 384). Cheap proxy = Skywork-Reward-V2-Qwen3-0.6B scalar reward score; within-group top half -> proxy=1; a separate global threshold (-2.266) gives proxy_pass positive rate 0.722 vs gold positive rate 0.722 (chosen to match). Expensive verifier = Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct (fallback_used=True), fixed 1-10 helpfulness+correctness rubric, temperature 0, max_tokens 8, gold = score>=7; judge output was unparseable (no digit in the 8-token completion) in 0.008% of rollouts, scored gold_positive=0 in that case. Group-level proxy_error = 1 if the group's top-RM response is not gold-positive. Files: avrl/runs/E09b/summary.json, result.json, feats.parquet, rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet, gen/judge/score_rm/analyze logs, sim_*.json, done.flag. Scripts: avrl/exp/E09_judge_task.py (gen/judge/score_rm stages, .venv-judge then .venv), avrl/exp/E09_analyze.py (.venv), avrl/e09_chain.sh (launcher).

## P1-judge-s0 2026-10-01 inconclusive (in-training test on the open-ended task, seed 0)
Hypothesis: on the weak-cheap-signal task (E09 family) the learned online allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than the fixed rule (verify the groups with the smallest top-two reward-model gap) at equal expensive-call budget, and the policy improves more.
Run: avrl/p1_judge_grpo.py on goldberg (A4000 16 GB, HF generation), Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct policy with LoRA r=32, prompts avrl/data/e09b_prompts.parquet (200 held out for eval), 8 prompts x G=6 per step, 320-token completions, 100 steps, budget 0.25 (2 of 8 groups verified per step), seed 0. Proxy reward: Skywork-Reward-V2-Qwen3-0.6B score above the group median. Expensive verifier and gold: Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct in 4-bit (the 7B does not fit beside training on this card), score >= 7. Logging-only judge calls (one per unverified group, for the corrupted count) are reported separately and not charged to the budget. Wall: 47 s/step, 78 min per arm, peak 12.8 GB.

| quantity | threshold (smallest RM gap first) | learned (online allocator) |
|---|---|---|
| expensive calls / groups | 200 / 800 | 200 / 800 |
| corrupted-reward groups admitted (of 600 unverified) | 52 (8.7%) | 54 (9.0%) |
| corrupted per 25-step window | 15, 9, 16, 12 | 15, 13, 14, 12 |
| eval judge score (mean of 200) at steps 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 | 4.58 / 4.58 / 4.59 / 4.59 | 4.58 / 4.55 / 4.59 / 4.59 |
| eval gold-positive rate | 0.46 throughout | 0.46 throughout |

Verdict: inconclusive. Corrupted admission is a tie (52 vs 54, well inside one seed's noise), and neither arm moved the held-out judge score in 100 steps, so there is no policy-improvement signal to compare. The replay edge of about +12 to +26 percent (L9a, L9b) did not appear in training at this scale. Possible reasons, all testable: too few steps for a LoRA policy to change judge-visible quality; a proxy reward with 50 percent positives per group gives GRPO little gradient; the 4-bit 3B judge as gold is noisy; the threshold rule on the RM gap is already a reasonable allocator. Follow-ups: seed 1 (queued), longer run with a larger learning rate and G=8 on an A100, and the E09d 7B-judge replay point for the curve.

Critic note (adversarial review, 2026-10-01): Verified every number in the ledger table and avrl/PROJECT.md's E09d row against avrl/runs/E09d/summary.json, result.json, judge_meta.json, gen_meta.json, feats.parquet, groups.parquet, rollouts.parquet, done.flag, the four sim_*.json files, and the gen/judge/score_rm/analyze logs. All match exactly: n_groups=4,000, n_proxy_error_groups=290, proxy_error_rate=0.0725, auc_cheap=0.79483, auc_cheap_emb=0.83527, auc_gap=0.04044, neighbor_ratio_cell100=1.14141, confident_subset n=1,000 error_rate=5.1% auc_cheap=0.7415 auc_cheap_emb=0.7853 neighbor_ratio_cell100=2.02441, judge_model_used=Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ fallback_used=False (loaded with enforce_eager=True per E09_judge_task.py line 78, the fix for E09's CUDA-OOM), frac_judge_unparseable=0.01563% (5/32,000). avrl/data/e09d_prompts.parquet has zero "<extra_id_" occurrences across all 4,000 prompts, confirming the markup-stripping fix from the E09 critic note held. The verdict rule is coded correctly (pass if auc_gap>=0.05 or neighbor_ratio_cell100>=1.5, else kill, since n_err=290>=60) and KILL is the correct mechanical output on the full-dataset statistics; this part of the entry is solid. No leakage: score_rm's rm_score function takes only (prompt, response) text; the cheap feature list (rm_max/mean/std/gap12, token stats, trunc_frac, log_qlen, consistency) never reads gold_score/gold_positive; proxy_error is used only as the y label; prompt embeddings are built from G.prompt only. Budget accounting is confirmed identical across all nine replay policies: runs/sim_generic.py computes budget = int(bf * len(sim)) once per budget fraction, outside the per-policy loop, and every policy's run()/run_online() call receives that same budget. All five pipeline stages exited 0 per done.flag; the "EngineCore died unexpectedly" lines in gen.log and judge.log occur after their respective summaries already printed, the same benign vLLM teardown noise flagged in every prior critic note on this family; row counts are preserved end to end (32,000 rollouts in and out of every stage, 4,000 groups in feats.parquet).

The confident-subset neighbor ratio of 2.02, which PROJECT.md calls "the first clear prompt-space structure in the project," gets closer scrutiny here since it is the strongest positive-sounding number this project has produced and is not itself part of the pre-registered pass/kill rule (that rule only checks the full-dataset neighbor_ratio_cell100=1.14, which fails). Recomputed directly from feats.parquet: the 1,000-group confident subset (top-quartile rm_gap12) carries 51 proxy-error groups spread across 31 of its 100 cell100 cells (never more than 5 errors in one cell, cell 64 with 19 members), reproducing D.neighbor_hit_ratio's 2.024408583492394 exactly. A permutation null (reshuffling the 51 error labels within the subset 1,000 times, cell100 membership fixed) gives mean 0.99, 95th percentile 1.386, max over 1,000 draws 1.735; 0 of 1,000 permutations reached or exceeded the observed 2.024 (empirical p<0.001). Leave-one-cell-out, dropping each of the 31 error-carrying cells one at a time, moves the ratio only between 1.994 (dropping cell 64, its single largest contributor) and 2.144 (dropping a 1-error cell); no single cell is carrying the result. So 2.02 is not a fluke of multiple testing or one hot cluster, and it is corroborated by an independent prior run: E09c (same 7B/AWQ judge, a different prompt sample via E09b's rollouts) already measured a confident-subset neighbor ratio of 1.84, while both 3B-fallback-judge runs (E09, E09b) measured 0.94 and 0.70 (no clustering at all). That is a real, judge-identity-correlated, twice-replicated pattern, not a one-off E09d artifact, and PROJECT.md's table and prose should say so instead of presenting 2.02 as unprecedented; as written it omits that E09c already cleared 1.5x on the same statistic.

That said, three things keep this from being a clean positive result. (1) Absolute power is thin: 51 events is below the project's own 60-event reliability floor used elsewhere for n_proxy_error_groups, even though the permutation test is sound at this n. (2) A hand-check of 10 random proxy-error groups (prompt, top-RM response, its judge score, the group's best-judged response) found: 1/10 (pid 256) is an "unwinnable" group where all 8 rollouts scored gold-negative, so proxy_error=1 regardless of proxy quality; unwinnable groups are 43/4,000 overall (1.1%) but 43/290 (14.8%) of all errors and 9/51 (17.6%) of confident-subset errors, so they are over-represented among errors. Quantifying the rest of the sample: the RM's top pick is a refusal-like response ("I'm sorry, I can't...") in only 5.75% of all groups (230/4,000), but those groups have a 22.2% proxy-error rate versus 6.3% elsewhere (3.5x), a real and mechanistically sensible failure mode (the RM penalizes refusals on average, mean rm_score -1.97 vs -0.28, but not always enough within a given group of 8 to keep them off the top). This accounts for 51/290 (17.6%) of all errors but only 7/51 (13.7%) of confident-subset errors, and a neighbor-ratio check on "RM top pick is a refusal" as its own label gives only 1.40 in the confident subset (lower than proxy_error's 2.02, and concentrated in only 5 of the 31 error-carrying cells), so refusal-clustering is a real contributor but does not fully explain the structure; 35/51 (68.6%) confident-subset errors are neither unwinnable nor refusal-driven, and the remaining hand-checked examples (pid 3520, 2197, 1185, 2641) look more like judge threshold noise (several groups have gold scores bunched at 4-7, straddling the >=7 cutoff) or the judge's inability to fact-check a confidently-worded hallucination than a clean quality gap. Net: proxy_error carries real signal, not pure judge noise, but it is a mix of at least three distinct mechanisms (unwinnable groups, a specific RM refusal-bias, and judge-threshold/fact-check noise), not a single clean construct. (3) Judge health is improved over E09's 3B fallback but not clean: of 32,000 scores, 29.1% are 7 and 48.3% are 8 (77.4% combined), while 9 is a round-number "valley" at only 1.6% (vs 4.1% for 10), the same coarse-rounding bias flagged before, now at the real 7B/AWQ judge rather than only the fallback. More importantly, this judge rewards truncation: mean gold_score 7.21 for truncated rollouts (37.2% of all 32,000) versus 6.92 for complete ones, and gold-positive rate 87.4% truncated versus 80.5% complete, while the RM scores truncation the opposite way (mean rm_score -0.75 truncated vs -0.21 complete). The original E09 critic note attributed this length/verbosity bias to being "specific to the fallback judge"; E09d shows the same qualitative bias with the real 7B judge running in eager mode, so that attribution was wrong and should be corrected: the bias is a property of this scoring setup (max_tokens=8 rubric, max_tokens=384 generation) in general, not the fallback model specifically.

The sim replay numbers are confirmed exactly: avrl/runs/E09d/sim_cell100_cheap.json budget_0.25 gives static=126.0, static_matched=102.625, greedy_online=121.875, ts_online=134.25, uniform=54.25, oracle=225.0, matching PROJECT.md's rounded 126.0/102.6/121.9/134.2/54.2/225 to the displayed decimal. What ts_online can exploit that greedy_online cannot: ts_online keeps a Beta posterior per cell100 cluster (reinforced online by a classifier-derived per-cell prior) and Thompson-samples which cell to visit next, so it can concentrate budget on whole clusters with a higher empirical hit rate even for member groups whose own cheap features look unremarkable; greedy_online has no notion of cluster at all and only re-ranks individual unseen groups by a single global self-trained classifier score, so it can only exploit per-group cheap-feature signal, never the between-cell variance the neighbor-ratio statistic measures. However, PROJECT.md's claim that E09d is "the first point where the bandit over prompt cells beats the greedy self-trainer" is stated as a finding in running prose while the same sentence admits "Bootstrap pending (L9d)": this rests on a single fit/sim split with 8 policy seeds, not the 300-group-resample bootstrap that L9a/L9b/L9c required before trusting any greedy-vs-ts_online comparison on this task family, and L9a's own bootstrapped result on E09 found the opposite ranking ("ts_online is weaker and less consistent than greedy_online... the plain self-trained ranking without Thompson layout over buckets, not the bucket-level exploration logic, is carrying this result"). Until L9d exists, this specific claim should be treated as an unconfirmed point estimate, not a project milestone, the same caution L9c applied to E09c's thinner point.

Verdict: the KILL label and every number in the E09d ledger entry are correct and reproducible; this is the most carefully executed point in the E09 family (real 7B/AWQ judge confirmed with no fallback, eager mode fixing the prior OOM, 4x the prompts, markup stripped) and its primary pre-registered result should be trusted as-is. The confident-subset neighbor ratio of 2.02 is a real, permutation-significant (p<0.001), non-single-cell-driven, twice-replicated-under-the-7B-judge effect, so it is more than noise. But PROJECT.md overstates it on two counts: it is presented as unprecedented when E09c already cleared the same bar (1.84), and the "bandit beats greedy self-trainer" framing is asserted ahead of the bootstrap confirmation this project's own methodology requires for that specific comparison, with a prior bootstrap (L9a) having found the opposite direction. E09d can bear real weight for "proxy error is not pure noise and has some exploitable concentration in a reward-model-proxy/strong-judge regime," but it cannot yet bear the weight of "claim 3 has a confirmed win" or "the bandit allocator is confirmed better than greedy" until L9d's bootstrap lands; both should be downgraded to provisional until then.


## L9d 2026-10-01 pass (E09d replay bootstrap, 4,000 prompts, 7B AWQ judge)
Run: avrl/exp/e09_bootstrap.py on avrl/runs/E09d/feats.parquet (4,000 groups, 290 error groups, 225 in the simulation split), 300 group resamples x 8 policy seeds, cell100, cheap features. Output avrl/runs/E09d/bootstrap.json.

| budget | greedy_online vs static_matched | 95% CI | ts_online (cell bandit) vs static_matched | 95% CI | greedy_online vs static (free labels) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10% | 66.6 vs 50.1, +32.9% | [+12.6%, +66.3%] | 68.9 vs 50.1, +37.4% | [+10.4%, +69.9%] | -11.2% [-25.1%, +38.6%] |
| 25% | 121.9 vs 102.6, +18.8% | [+7.8%, +26.1%] | 134.2 vs 102.6, +30.8% | [+13.6%, +36.6%] | -3.3% [-15.3%, +17.3%] |
| 50% | 192.1 vs 170.8, +12.5% | [+2.6%, +11.3%] | 194.2 vs 170.8, +13.8% | [+2.6%, +15.4%] | +4.4% [-7.3%, +8.1%] |

Verdict: pass at every budget (rule: CI on the 25 percent relative gain over the budget-matched threshold excludes zero on the positive side). This is the well-powered point for the weak-cheap-signal regime: cheap AUC 0.80, embeddings add 0.040, confident-subset neighbor ratio 2.02 (critic: permutation p < 0.001, 51 error events, mechanistically mixed). The cell bandit's point estimates exceed the greedy self-trainer's at every budget but the intervals overlap, so the bandit-versus-greedy ordering remains provisional. Neither learned policy beats a threshold given free labels, so the claim is about equal label budgets.
What it changes: with E01, E06, E08 (strong cheap signal, gain about zero) and E09b and E09d (weak cheap signal, gain +19 to +31 percent at 25 percent budget), the structure-statistic-versus-gain relation the proposal makes central has its first two regimes with confidence intervals. The open question is whether the replay edge appears in training: P1-judge-s0 (100 steps, 4-bit 3B judge on the 16 GB card) was a tie; a powered run (A100, 7B judge, G=8, 300 steps) is the decisive next experiment.

## P1-judge-s1 2026-10-01 inconclusive (replication of the in-training tie, seed 1)
Run: identical to P1-judge-s0 with --seed 1. Files: avrl/runs/p1_judge_threshold_s1/, avrl/runs/p1_judge_learned_s1/.

| quantity | threshold s0 / s1 | learned s0 / s1 |
|---|---|---|
| expensive calls (of 800 groups) | 200 / 200 | 200 / 200 |
| corrupted-reward groups admitted (of 600) | 52 / 49 | 54 / 50 |
| corrupted per 25-step window | 15,9,16,12 / 11,12,10,16 | 15,13,14,12 / 9,17,9,15 |
| eval judge score at steps 25 to 100 | 4.58 to 4.59 / 4.465 flat | 4.58 to 4.59 / 4.465 flat |

Verdict: inconclusive, replicated. Corrupted admission is a tie in both seeds (within 3 groups of 600), and the held-out judge score does not change at all during training in either arm, which means these runs contain no policy-improvement signal to compare and the allocator comparison reduces to the replay. A perfectly constant eval score across four evaluations is being checked for an evaluation bug (whether the evaluator decodes from the live adapter); if the evaluator is correct, the LoRA policy is not changing its greedy outputs in 100 steps at this learning rate, and the powered A100 run (hannah job 78882, 150 steps, G=8, 7B judge) is the decisive test.

## Note 2026-10-01: root cause of the flat in-training evaluations
Both evaluators decode from the live model (kwargs["model"] in the TrainerCallback), so the flat judge scores in P1-judge-s0 and s1 are not an evaluator bug. The LoRA adapters barely moved: lora_B norm 0.033 after 100 steps on the open-ended task (0.041 on GSM8K, which moved accuracy about 3 points), with learning_rate 1e-6 and linear decay to zero, an order of magnitude below the usual LoRA range. On a task scored by a coarse judge this is invisible. Both harnesses now take --lr (default 1e-6 for reproducibility). Reruns at 1e-5: goldberg pair (threshold, learned; runs p1_judge_*_lr5) and hannah job 78883 (four arms, bf16 7B judge, 150 steps). All P1 entries above should be read as "at lr 1e-6"; the GSM8K claim-2 kills (P1-goldberg-s0, s1) stand on corrupted admission, which does not depend on policy movement, but the accuracy ties do.

## M01 2026-10-01 inconclusive (script's own tally), corrected below to kill

Hypothesis: the two-statistic regime diagnostic (cheap-feature AUC >= 0.85 -> STRONG -> predict threshold wins; AUC < 0.85 -> WEAK -> predict a learned/structure-aware allocator wins) correctly labels which allocation policy actually won, using only each point's already-measured cheap-feature AUC and neighbor-ratio statistics, across the nine points in the ledger (E01/p0, E04/L5a, E05, E06, E08, E09, E09b, E09c, E09d).

Run: no new rollouts, no GPU, CPU-only, sub-second runtime. Script avrl/exp/M01.py (new file, does not edit any p0/E0N/L-series script) reads each point's own result.json under avrl/runs/<point>/, applies the 0.85 AUC cutoff, and compares the predicted winner against each point's recorded ledger verdict. Files checked directly (not just the run report): avrl/runs/M01/result.json, run.log (byte-identical), done.flag. n_groups per point verified against each point's own result.json: p0=3000, L5a=3000, E05=3000, E06=3000, E08=600, E09=1500, E09b=1500, E09c=1500, E09d=4000.

| point | n_groups | auc_cheap | regime | predicted | actual_winner (as labeled by script) | match | ground-truth source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E01/p0 | 3000 | 0.923 | STRONG | threshold | threshold | MATCH | bootstrap (L3a/L4a, CI includes zero at 10/25/50%) |
| E04/L5a | 3000 | 0.815 | WEAK | allocator | threshold | MISMATCH | qualitative (L7b, ranking-signal test, not a policy replay; no feats.parquet for this point) |
| E05 | 3000 | 0.927 | STRONG | threshold | threshold | MATCH | qualitative (L6a, ranking-signal test, not a policy replay; no feats.parquet for this point) |
| E06 | 3000 | 0.887 | STRONG | threshold | threshold | MATCH (contested, see below) | qualitative, inferred from L6a/L7b's unrelated ranking-signal citation |
| E08 | 600 | 0.868 | STRONG | threshold | threshold | MATCH (contested, see below) | qualitative, inferred from E08's prose about the free-label "static" baseline, not static_matched |
| E09 | 1500 | 0.734 | WEAK | allocator | allocator | MATCH | bootstrap (L9a, PASS, 25% CI [+3.9%, +25.5%]) |
| E09b | 1500 | 0.746 | WEAK | allocator | allocator | MATCH | bootstrap (L9b, PASS, 25% CI [+5.3%, +26.2%]) |
| E09c | 1500 | 0.799 | WEAK | allocator | threshold | MISMATCH | bootstrap (L9c, INCONCLUSIVE, CI [-5.7%, +33.7%] includes zero -> tie) |
| E09d | 4000 | 0.795 | WEAK | allocator | allocator | MATCH | bootstrap (L9d, PASS, 25% CI [+7.8%, +26.1%]) |

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds, as the script computes it: n_match=7/9, n_mismatch=2/9. Pass required n_match>=8/9; kill required n_mismatch>=3/9. Neither bound is hit mechanically, so the script's own output is INCONCLUSIVE.

Critic note (M01, appended in the same pass): independently re-derived every ground-truth label against its cited RESEARCH_LOG.md section rather than trusting result.json's citation field. E01/p0, E09, E09b, E09c, E09d check out exactly: all five have an actual bootstrap CI on greedy_online or ts_online vs static_matched, and the labels match those CIs' pass/kill/inconclusive calls verbatim. E04/L5a and E05 have no replay data at all (no feats.parquet, confirmed by directory listing), so L6a/L7b's ranking-signal test (which cheap feature to sort by, not which allocation policy to run) is the only evidence on disk and the "qualitative" flag on these two is a fair caveat.

E06 and E08 are different: both DO have their own already-computed avrl/runs/<point>/sim_cell100_cheap.json allocation-replay file, the same file every bootstrap-backed point's bootstrap script sanity-checks itself against (see L9a's own text). Reading those two files directly: at the project's standard primary config (cell100, cheap features, 25% budget), greedy_online beats static_matched on E06 (437.0 vs 361.5 hits, +20.9%) and on E08 (81.6 vs 75.4 hits, +8.3%); at 50% budget the same direction holds for both (E06 +9.2%, E08 +11.6%); only at 10% budget does static_matched lead on both (E06 -23.7% for greedy_online relative, i.e. static_matched ahead; E08 similarly). No bootstrap CI was ever run on either point's greedy_online-vs-static_matched gap, so statistical significance is unverified, but the only evidence that exists points the opposite direction from the "threshold wins" label the script assigned, which was instead inferred from E08's prose about the free-label "static" baseline ("learned online allocators never clear the free-label static rule") and from L6a/L7b's unrelated self-verification/judge ranking-signal tests for E06 -- neither citation is actually about greedy_online vs static_matched.

Corrected verdict: if E06 and E08 are treated as contested rather than clean matches (the only available evidence for both points favors the allocator, not the threshold, at the project's own primary budget), n_match drops from 7/9 to 5/9 and n_mismatch rises from 2/9 to 4/9, clearing the pre-registered kill bar (n_mismatch>=3/9). Reading this plainly: KILL, not inconclusive. The 0.85 single-cutoff diagnostic, as currently specified, does not reliably predict which policy wins once ground truth is checked against each point's own best-available evidence rather than against inferred citations from a different kind of test.

What it changes: this is the gating check for the entire AVRL-Router product concept (M02-M05 in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md), which depends on the step-0 regime switch being trustworthy. The mechanical script result (7/9, "one off from the pass bar") understates how shaky this is: only 5 of 9 points have a real bootstrap CI behind their ground-truth label at all, and 2 of the 4 qualitative points (E06, E08) have existing, cheap-to-read evidence that contradicts their assigned label rather than merely being weakly evidenced. Before any GPU time goes into M02-M05, the diagnostic itself needs either a corrected ground truth or a recalibrated cutoff, not just a rerun with the same inputs.

Follow-ups:
1. (L10a, ~15-30 min, CPU-only, reuses E06/E08's existing feats.parquet) Run avrl/exp/e09_bootstrap.py's exact methodology (cell100, cheap features, greedy_online vs static_matched, 300 resamples x 8 seeds) on E06 and E08, the two points that currently have a point estimate but no CI. This directly settles whether E06/E08 belong in the threshold-wins bucket or the allocator-wins bucket, resolving the single biggest open question this entry raises, with no new generation needed.
2. (L10b, ~10 min, CPU-only, depends on L10a) Rerun M01.py's diagnose_all logic with E06 and E08's ground-truth labels corrected to whatever L10a's CI actually shows (rather than the current inferred citations), and recompute the 9-point pass/kill tally. This tells us definitively whether the corrected diagnostic clears 8/9 (pass), 3+ mismatches (kill), or lands in a genuinely different inconclusive zone, replacing this entry's provisional "corrected to kill" read with a number backed by the same bootstrap standard used for the other 5 points.
3. (L10c, ~5 min, CPU-only, reuses already-computed AUCs) Sweep the regime cutoff from 0.75 to 0.90 in steps of 0.025 against the corrected (post-L10a) ground truth and plot match count per cutoff. The current 0.85 line sits close to several contested points (E06 0.887, E08 0.868, L5a 0.815); this checks whether the diagnostic's failure is a cutoff-placement problem (fixable by recalibration) or whether no single AUC threshold separates the two outcome groups cleanly (meaning the single-statistic router-switch design itself, not just its tuning, is the problem), before deciding whether M02-M05 are worth funding in any form.

Critic note (independent re-verification pass, 2026-10-01): re-checked every number in this entry against the raw files (avrl/runs/M01/result.json and run.log, each of the nine points' own result.json, and E06/E08's sim_cell100_cheap.json plus analyze.log). All nine auc_cheap values, all nine n_groups values, the primary 7/9 mechanical tally, the pass/kill thresholds (>=8/9 match, >=3/9 mismatch), the equal-budget accounting across policies (budget = int(fraction x len(sim)), identical for every policy in sim_alloc.py), and the 8-seed-per-policy sample size (SEEDS=8, byte-identical sim_alloc.py in every run dir) all check out exactly. Cheap-feature lists for every point (agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen, plus code-specific syntax/exception fractions for E08) are derived only from the model's own rollouts, never from the gold answer, so no leakage into auc_cheap or the allocator features.

One correction to this entry's own E06/E08 contested-evidence paragraph: it claims "only at 10% budget does static_matched lead on both (E06 -23.7% for greedy_online relative, i.e. static_matched ahead for E06; E08 similarly)." This is wrong for E06. Reading E06's sim_cell100_cheap.json and analyze.log table directly: at 10% budget static_matched=148.25 and greedy_online=183.375, i.e. greedy_online leads by +23.7%, the same direction as at 25% (+20.9%) and 50% (+9.2%). Greedy_online beats static_matched at every budget tested for E06, not just two of three. For E08 the "static_matched leads at 10%" claim is directionally correct but the magnitude is off: static_matched=31.375 vs greedy_online=22.25 at 10% budget is a -29.1% deficit for greedy_online, not -23.7% (the -23.7% figure appears to be E06's magnitude misattributed to both points with the sign flipped for E06).

This does not change the entry's corrected verdict, it strengthens the E06 half of it: E06 is not merely "contested," the only evidence that exists (uncontested across all three budgets) favors the allocator, directly contradicting the "threshold" label the diagnostic inferred from an unrelated ranking-signal citation. E08 remains genuinely mixed (threshold ahead at 10%, allocator ahead at 25% and 50%, the project's own primary-reporting budget per L9a-d). With E06 and E08 both corrected to allocator at the 25%-budget standard, n_match=5/9 and n_mismatch=4/9, clearing the pre-registered kill bar (n_mismatch>=3/9).

Corrected verdict: KILL. The 0.85 cheap-AUC cutoff does not reliably predict which allocator wins once every point's ground truth is checked against its own best-available evidence (bootstrap CI where one exists, direct allocation-replay read where it does not) rather than against inferred citations from unrelated ranking-signal tests. Only 5 of 9 points have a real bootstrap CI behind their label, and both points with cheap-to-read but uncited replay evidence (E06, E08) contradict the label the citation-based scheme assigned them. Per the pre-registered rule, this is a kill: the single-statistic router-switch design (M02-M05) needs either a recalibrated ground truth and cutoff, or should not receive further GPU time in its current form, before L10a/L10b/L10c are worth running.
