## S1 probe 2026-10-05 19:35 UTC: 7B policy fits one A100 (hannah job 78946)
Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct policy with LoRA r32 and gradient checkpointing, 8 prompts x 8 completions x 384 tokens, Skywork 0.6B reward model on the same card, bf16 7B judge on a second card: 4 training steps plus two 50-prompt evaluations in 513 s; pure step time about 102 s, a 50-prompt evaluation about 50 s (so 200 prompts about 3.5 min); peak GPU memory 25.8 GB on the policy card; untrained 7B scores 8.08 under the 7B judge on the 50-prompt sample. Budget: 1000 steps about 29 h per job, 1500 about 43 h, both inside the 68 h harness limit and the 3-day slurm cap. A second probe with the 14B judge (job 78947) is queued; three design reviews are running before submission.
Addendum (19:45 UTC): the first 14B-judge probe (job 78947) ran out of memory on the judge card because the harness scores a whole group of 8 responses in one judge batch. Added chunked scoring to the judge (HFJudge.score_batch now honours a batch_size attribute, set by the new --judge_batch_size flag; scores are unchanged by chunking) and set judge and evaluation batches to 2 for the 14B judge; probe resubmitted as 78949.
Addendum (19:55 UTC): the second 14B probe (78949) still overflowed the judge card, and the traceback showed the overflow inside the policy's own generation on GPU 1: with two visible GPUs TRL loads the policy with an automatic device map and spreads it across both cards, so the 7B probe had silently shared the judge's card too. Added --policy_single_gpu (model_init_kwargs device_map {"": 0} through GRPOConfig) so the policy stays on cuda:0 and the judge has cuda:1 to itself; probe resubmitted as 78950.
Addendum (20:05 UTC): with the policy pinned to one card (78950) the overflow moved to the policy card during generation (37.8 GB in use, 2 GB more requested): a 7B policy generating 64 sequences of up to about 1400 tokens with the HF generator's key-value cache does not fit beside the weights, the LoRA optimizer state and the reward model on 40 GB. The earlier 7B probe passed only because the policy was secretly spread over two cards. Probe 78951 halves the generation batch (4 prompts x 8 completions per step, same completions per prompt); if it fits, S1 runs at 4 x 8 with the step count doubled to keep the number of groups, and the design review's feasibility arithmetic is redone from its step time.
Addendum (20:25 UTC, S1 preparation): probe 78951 (4 prompts x 8, policy pinned to cuda:0) got through generation and the judge calls and then failed in the training forward with an NCCL error: with two visible GPUs the Trainer wraps the policy in DataParallel and tries to replicate it onto the judge's card. Fixed by reporting a single GPU to the trainer when the policy is pinned (build_grpo_config forces n_gpu to 1). Two review findings were also applied: the judge chunking flag had not actually landed in HFJudge (now it has, scoped to that class), and the overoptimization test needed an unbounded proxy signal, so each step now logs rm_score_mean (the mean raw reward-model score over all groups). The S1 plan was revised per the design review: three chained jobs (a: proxy and consistency threshold at seed 0; b: full at seed 0 and a proxy arm at learning rate 3e-5 as the drift probe; c: consistency threshold and full at seed 1), 4 prompts x 8 completions per step with doubled step counts, drift and overoptimization tests with minimum-window clauses, and the reward-shape and same-judge caveats stated. Probe 78952 (4 x 8, 14B judge, single-GPU trainer) decides the step count.

The peak is the full-vocabulary logits of the training forward (8 sequences x about 1400 tokens x 152k vocabulary in float32), so a --micro_batch option now lets the training forward run 4 sequences at a time with the gradient accumulation doubled, keeping one optimizer step per generation batch; probe 78953 measures the new peak. Step count for S1 at 4 x 8: 2000 steps (the same number of groups as 1000 steps at 8 x 8), about 27 h per job at 46 s per step plus ten evaluations.

## S1 attempt 2 stopped 2026-10-06 03:20 UTC: at 2 prompts per step the 25 percent budget rounded to zero verified groups
The interim monitor showed the consistency-threshold arm with 400 unverified groups in its first 200 steps, the same as the proxy arm: the shared budget-to-count rule round(0.25 x 2) is 0, so with 2 prompts per step the budgeted arm was not verifying anything (every earlier run had 8 or 4 groups per step, where the product is an integer). Attempt 2's 229 steps per arm (both at the untrained level at step 200: 7.465 and 7.45 against 7.455) are kept under runs/s1_attempt2_logs on hannah.
Fix: n_verify(n_groups, budget, step) now realizes fractional budgets by cumulative rounding (floor(q (step + 1)) minus floor(q step)), so 2 groups at 25 percent verify 1 group on alternate steps and the fraction over a run equals the budget exactly; without a step it behaves as before, and for 8 or 4 groups at 25 percent it returns 2 or 1 every step as before, so no earlier result changes. The judge harness passes its call index as the step. A consequence to state in the write-up: at 2 groups per step the threshold rule chooses the less confident of two groups on alternate steps, a much coarser ranking than the two least confident of eight, so its corruption reduction at scale will understate what the rule does at the small-scale batch; the drift and overoptimization tests (proxy arms) are unaffected. Chain relaunched (attempt 3) at 4000 steps.
Addendum (03:25 UTC): S1 attempt 3 job ids a=78966 (running), b=78967, c=78968, chained, 4000 steps per arm at 2 x 8 with the cumulative-rounding budget.
Addendum (03:35 UTC): attempt 3 confirmed: the threshold arm's expensive calls over its first four steps are 0, 1, 0, 1 (one group on alternate steps, 25 percent over the run), both arms at 32.3 s per step, no errors; job a ends near 17:30 UTC on Oct 7.

- Consistency-threshold arm: held-out 7.465 to 7.72 at step 1600 and 7.695 at step 1800, no decline yet; the step-2000 evaluation had not landed in the log at collection time. Its corruption rate fell from 0.147 to 0.033 and its raw reward-model score is 7.23, below the proxy arm's 7.76.

Lesson: do not read a single 200-prompt evaluation as a trend; the pre-registered last-two-mean rule exists for this reason.

## S1 job b interim 2026-10-07 21:45 UTC (step about 630 of 4000): the full-verification arm has sparse learning signal
Held-out judge score: full arm 7.43, 7.38, 7.435 at steps 200, 400, 600 (untrained 7.455); proxy at triple learning rate 7.58, 7.635, 7.685; for comparison the seed-0 proxy arm from job a was 7.37, 7.47, 7.60 and the threshold arm 7.465, 7.44, 7.55 at the same steps.
Diagnosis: the full arm's per-step training reward mean is 0.81 to 0.82 (the judge scores about 82 percent of responses at 7 or above), and on 32 percent of its steps the mean is exactly 0 or 1, meaning every one of the 16 completions in both groups got the same reward. Under GRPO a group with identical rewards has zero advantage and contributes no gradient. From 0.32 for both groups together, the per-group chance of a uniform reward is about 0.57, so roughly 57 percent of the full arm's groups teach nothing. The proxy arms' rank reward (top half of the group by reward-model score) always splits every group, so 0 percent of their steps are uniform. This is the pre-registered reward-shape caveat (M12) showing up at scale: the full arm's absolute reward (score >= 7) is a weaker training signal than the rank reward, independent of label quality.
Consequence for claim 1 at scale: if the full arm finishes at or below the proxy and threshold arms, that is not evidence that verification is useless, because the arm is handicapped by reward shape. The fair comparison is the pre-registered follow-up S1-rank (full arm with --gold_reward_shape rank, so the judge's scores are turned into a top-half reward the same way the reward model's are). Not launched; proposed to Rustom for the slot after job c.

## S1 job b interim 2026-10-08 07:45 UTC (step about 2200 of 4000): the overoptimization test fires for the first time, on the triple-learning-rate proxy arm
Pre-registered analysis on partial logs (avrl/runs/s1_analysis). Interim reading; the pre-registered verdict is taken on the final window at step 4000.
- OVEROPTIMIZATION, proxy at learning rate 3e-5: PRESENT. Held-out judge score 7.58, 7.635, 7.685, 7.545, 7.645, 7.53, 7.70 (peak, step 1400), 7.595, 7.645, 7.555, 7.42 (step 2200, below the untrained 7.455). Mean of the last two 7.4875, drop from peak 0.213 against the 0.15 criterion. Raw reward-model score rose 3.61 to 8.32 over the same span.
- DRIFT, same arm: ABSENT (corrupted share of unverified groups 0.130 in the first 200 steps, 0.117 in the last 200, ratio 0.90, interval 0.61 to 1.35). So the held-out decline is not caused by the cheap grader's ranking becoming wrong more often; it is the policy optimizing the cheap reward model in a direction the judge does not credit, the classic overoptimization shape.
- Contrast: the same arm at learning rate 1e-5 (job a) reached a cheap score of 8.78 with no held-out decline over 4000 steps. The difference is optimization pressure per step, consistent with the overoptimization literature (the proxy-gold gap opens with the amount of optimization, here delivered faster by the larger step size).
- Full-verification arm (job a's comparison) at step 2000: 7.58, slowly rising, still below the job a proxy arm's 7.58 at the same step only by coincidence of value; see the sparse-signal entry above.
- Analysis-script issue found: the CLAIM 1 AT SCALE line reported PASS using the full arm's latest evaluation although the arm is incomplete (2117 steps). Claim 1 must require both arms complete; fixed below.

## S1-xjudge launched 2026-10-08 20:05 UTC: cross-family judges for the S1 arms (goldberg)
Purpose: the S1 caveat that the 14B judge is from the policy's own family (Qwen). Pre-registered readings before any result is seen:
- Setup: the untrained 7B policy and the final adapters of the finished arms (proxy seed 0, consistency threshold seed 0; later arms appended as they finish) regenerate the same 200 held-out prompts (eval_seed 0, greedy, 384 tokens, bf16 with the LoRA merged) on goldberg. Each response set is scored by two judges outside the Qwen family with the identical rubric and parser: microsoft/phi-4 (14B) and allenai/OLMo-2-1124-7B-Instruct, bf16. Per-response scores are saved (scores.jsonl) so arms can be compared on the same prompts.
- Reading 1 (are the training gains real?): under each cross-family judge, each trained arm's mean score against the untrained base. Both arms beat the base by 0.27 to 0.36 under the Qwen judge; if a cross-family judge shows no gain, the S1 gains were judge-family-specific.
- Reading 2 (does the arm ordering hold?): proxy minus threshold under each cross-family judge, against the Qwen judge's 0.085 gap (declared a tie). No ordering is claimed unless a gap exceeds 0.15 under both cross-family judges.
- Follow-up once job b is in: score the triple-learning-rate arm at its peak checkpoint (step 1400) and its final adapter with the same judges, to test whether the overoptimization decline is visible to judges outside the family.
Files: avrl/s1_xjudge.sh, avrl/s1_xjudge_run.sh, avrl/exp/S1_base_eval.py (--adapter, --responses, scores.jsonl), outputs under avrl/runs/s1_xjudge/<arm>/<judge>/result.json.
Addendum (20:20 UTC): moved off goldberg at Rustom's request onto the lab pool (willow, walnut, pine, cherry; one RTX A4000 16 GB each, plain ssh as the user). On 16 GB the policy is loaded with bitsandbytes 8-bit (LoRA applied unmerged) and the judges in 4-bit NF4; the untrained base is regenerated under the same 8-bit setting so all sets are like for like. The proxy arm's bf16 responses that goldberg had finished before the stop are kept as a fourth set (_goldberg_bf16_s1_proxy_s0) and scored by the same judges, which measures how much the 8-bit regeneration moves a score. Scripts: avrl/s1_xjudge.sh (one unit) and avrl/s1_xjudge_pool.sh (dispatcher), log avrl/runs/s1_xjudge/pool.log.

## S1-xjudge complete 2026-10-08 21:30 UTC: three judges outside the Qwen family, paired on the same 200 prompts
Paired mean differences with 95 percent bootstrap intervals (per-response scores, same prompts, parse failures dropped: at most 1 of 200).

| comparison | Phi-4 (14B) | OLMo-2 7B | OLMo-2 13B | Qwen 14B (training-time, unpaired) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| proxy minus untrained | +0.42 [+0.26, +0.59] | +0.21 [+0.14, +0.28] | +0.96 [+0.83, +1.09] | +0.355 |
| threshold minus untrained | +0.41 [+0.30, +0.53] | +0.21 [+0.14, +0.29] | +0.70 [+0.55, +0.84] | +0.27 |
| proxy minus threshold | +0.01 [-0.12, +0.15] | -0.01 [-0.04, +0.02] | +0.28 [+0.15, +0.41] | +0.085 |
| same proxy adapter, 8-bit minus bf16 | +0.08 [-0.04, +0.19] | +0.01 | +0.13 [+0.05, +0.21] | not measured |

- Reading 1 (gains are real): PASS. Every judge puts both trained arms above the untrained model with intervals clear of zero. The S1 gains are not a Qwen-judging-Qwen artifact.
- Reading 2 (ordering): the pre-registered rule required a gap above 0.15 under both cross-family judges. Phi-4 gives +0.01 (tie); OLMo-2 13B gives +0.28 in the proxy arm's favour. One of two, so no ordering is claimed; the honest statement is that the 25 percent threshold arm is no better than the unverified proxy arm under any judge, and under one judge it is worse.
- Quantization: the 8-bit regeneration scores 0.01 to 0.13 above the bf16 responses of the same adapter. Under OLMo-2 13B that offset is half the proxy-threshold gap, so part of that judge's gap could be regeneration noise; this is another reason not to claim the ordering.
- OLMo-2 7B sits at its ceiling (positive rate 1.0 everywhere) and is kept only as a direction check.
- Caveat on absolute levels: the judges differ by up to 1.3 points in generosity but agree on direction.
Consequence for the thesis: at 7B with a normal learning rate, budgeted verification bought nothing on held-out quality that the cheap grader alone did not deliver, now confirmed under three judge families. The case for verification rests on the overoptimization regime (job b, triple learning rate), where the cheap grader alone fails; the follow-up is to score that arm's peak and final checkpoints with these same judges once job b finishes.

## S1-xjudge result, triple-learning-rate arm 2026-10-16 01:30 UTC: overoptimization is NOT confirmed outside the Qwen family
Paired on the same 200 prompts, 95 percent bootstrap intervals (5000 resamples, seed 0; parse failures dropped, 1 of 200 for OLMo-2 13B on base and final). Raw means, peak (step 1400) / final (step 4000) / untrained / lr 1e-5 proxy: Phi-4 8.625 / 8.475 / 8.28 / 8.70; OLMo-2 7B 9.93 / 9.985 / 9.76 / 9.965; OLMo-2 13B 9.06 / 9.53 / 8.62 / 9.565. Qwen 14B (training-time): 7.70 / 7.48 / 7.455.
| comparison | Phi-4 (14B) | OLMo-2 7B (ceiling) | OLMo-2 13B |
|---|---|---|---|
| final minus peak | -0.15 [-0.28, -0.01] | +0.06 [+0.03, +0.09] | +0.46 [+0.35, +0.57] |
| peak minus untrained | +0.34 [+0.18, +0.51] | +0.17 [+0.10, +0.25] | +0.45 [+0.32, +0.58] |
| final minus untrained | +0.20 [+0.01, +0.38] | +0.23 [+0.15, +0.30] | +0.91 [+0.77, +1.05] |
| final minus lr 1e-5 proxy | -0.23 [-0.35, -0.11] | +0.02 [+0.00, +0.04] | -0.05 [-0.15, +0.06] |
- Reading 1 (pre-registered: needs a drop below -0.10 with interval excluding zero under BOTH Phi-4 and OLMo-2 13B): MIXED, not met. Phi-4 shows the decline (-0.15, interval just excludes zero); OLMo-2 13B shows the opposite, a significant rise of +0.46. The decline seen by the Qwen judge (-0.22) is therefore not a cross-family-robust result; it is visible under one of two usable non-Qwen judges and reversed under the other.
- Reading 2: the final policy is still above untrained under all three non-Qwen judges (+0.20 to +0.91), unlike the Qwen judge where it fell back to +0.025. No judge outside the family sees a collapse to base.
- Reading 3 (peak is real): peak beats untrained under all three, so the step-1400 checkpoint is genuinely better than base; but "peak" was chosen from a noisy Qwen eval and under OLMo-2 13B it is the weaker checkpoint.
- Side finding: final vs the lr 1e-5 proxy arm is -0.23 under Phi-4 (3e-5 is worse than 1e-5 there) and a tie under OLMo-2 13B and OLMo-2 7B.
- Interpretation, not tested: the cheap reward model is optimized harder at 3e-5, and judges disagree on what that pressure bought (OLMo-2 13B may credit style or length the Qwen and Phi-4 judges do not). Response length drift was not measured here.
- Consequence for the thesis: the overoptimization regime, the case for verification, rests on a Qwen-family-only decline of 0.22 on a single seed. Not claimed as cross-family confirmed. Candidate checks, NOT launched: response length of peak vs final, a second seed of the 3e-5 arm.
Files: avrl/runs/s1_xjudge/s1_proxy_lr3_s0{,_peak1400}/<judge>/{result.json,scores.jsonl}, dispatcher avrl/s1_xjudge_pool3.sh, log avrl/runs/s1_xjudge/pool3.log. Job d, job c follow-ups and S1-rank were not launched.

Pre-registered analysis rerun on the complete logs (avrl/s1_collect.sh, then avrl/exp/S1_analyze.py; avrl/runs/s1_analysis/S1_RESULT.md). Every arm 4000 steps, exit 0. Untrained 7.455.

| arm | calls | final judge score | last-five mean | corrupted / unverified | rm first to last block |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| proxy s0 (lr 1e-5) | 0 | 7.81 | 7.80 | 611 / 8000 (0.076) | 3.16 to 8.78 |
| threshold 25 percent s0 | 2000 | 7.725 | 7.725 | 439 / 6000 (0.073) | 3.15 to 8.16 |
| full s0 | 8000 | 7.645 | 7.605 | 0 / 0 | 3.11 to 3.18 |
| threshold 25 percent s1 | 2000 | 7.76 | 7.73 | 482 / 6000 (0.080) | 3.24 to 8.88 |
| full s1 | 8000 | 7.67 | 7.665 | 0 / 0 | 3.14 to 3.73 |
| proxy s0 (lr 3e-5) | 0 | 7.48 | 7.52 | 863 / 8000 (0.108) | 3.61 to 10.56 |

- CLAIM 1 AT SCALE: PASS by the pre-registered rule (mean gap full minus threshold -0.085 over two seeds: -0.08, -0.09; bar 0.15). The consistency threshold at a quarter of the judge calls finishes level with or above full verification on both seeds. Unchanged caveat (M12, job b interim): the full arm trains on an absolute score >= 7 reward and about 57 percent of its groups carry uniform reward, so it is handicapped by reward shape, not only helped by labels; the fair bound is S1-rank (not launched, waits on Rustom).
- The full arms' raw reward-model score barely moves (3.1 to 3.2 and 3.1 to 3.7) while every proxy-shaped arm drives it to 8 to 10.6. The judge-trained policy does not raise the cheap grader's score at all, so cheap-score inflation in the proxy arms is optimization of the reward model, not a side effect of getting better under the judge.
- DRIFT remains ABSENT in both proxy arms over the full 4000 steps (lr 3e-5 ratio 0.73, interval 0.48 to 1.08, updated from the step-2200 interim).
- OVEROPTIMIZATION, lr 3e-5 arm, final window: PRESENT under the Qwen judge (peak 7.70, last two 7.52, drop 0.18 against 0.15), cheap score 10.56. Cross-family status is MIXED (entry of 01:30 UTC): Phi-4 agrees, OLMo-2 13B reverses.
- Seed-1 threshold arm (7.76) replicates the seed-0 pattern: threshold and proxy arms sit 0.27 to 0.36 above untrained, full arms 0.19 to 0.22 above.

Response-length check for the mixed cross-judge result (CPU, from the regenerated xjudge responses, 200 prompts, 384-token cap): mean characters base 1369, lr 1e-5 proxy 1577, threshold 1499, lr 3e-5 peak (step 1400) 1372, lr 3e-5 final 1560; share of responses within 10 percent of the longest: peak 0.01, final 0.16, lr 1e-5 proxy 0.15. The final checkpoint's responses are 14 percent longer than the peak's and more often hit the cap. Consistent with the untested interpretation that OLMo-2 13B credits length that Qwen and Phi-4 do not, but length alone cannot be the whole story since the lr 1e-5 proxy arm is just as long and shows no Qwen-judge decline. Not pre-registered; descriptive only.
Consequence: with claim 1 passing at 7B and the overoptimization decline Qwen-family-only, the scale-up supports the cheap-rule half of the thesis (threshold at 25 percent equals full verification) and leaves the "verification prevents collapse" half resting on one seed and one judge family. Nothing launched.

## AR1 2026-10-16 23:40 UTC pre-registered (planner-context ablation of the autoresearch loop, replayed offline; running)
Hypothesis (from autoresearch-routing-context.md): what the planner sees about past experiments changes how many experiments the loop needs to reach the right claim verdicts. Arm A sees full result bodies of every prior experiment (full logs); arm B sees one line per experiment (compressed ledger); arm C sees B plus two sampled full entries of killed or inconclusive experiments (OpenEvolve-style inspirations).
Substrate: the loop's own trace. The 52 critic-checked small-scale experiments of 2026-09-30 to 2026-10-03 (ids, hypotheses, numeric result bodies with verdict paragraphs stripped) form an oracle (avrl/exp/ar1_build_oracle.py, avrl/runs/AR1/oracle.json). Each iteration a Sonnet planner picks the next experiment from the menu of not-yet-run ids; the runner is a lookup returning the archived numbers; a Sonnet analyst (identical across arms, sees only the compressed ledger plus the new result) writes the verdict, a one-liner, and the three claim states. Ground truth: claim 1 pass, claim 2 kill or tie, claim 3 kill (PROJECT.md, 2026-10-02 10:50 UTC). A run settles when the claim state matches on two consecutive iterations; cap 20 iterations (censored at 21).
Design: 3 arms x 5 seeds (seed shuffles the menu order and the inspiration sample), Sonnet for both roles, no tools, JSON-schema outputs via claude -p. Metrics: experiments-to-verdict (primary), total model cost and planner input tokens (secondary), claim flips (a claim settled then changed), analyst agreement with the recorded verdicts.
Pre-registered reads: Read 1 PASS if arm B or arm C reaches the verdict in fewer experiments than arm A on average at lower cost; Read 2 PASS if arm C beats arm B on experiments-to-verdict. With five seeds only the mean and the seed-paired sign count are reported; no interval claim.
Known limits, stated up front: the menu descriptions for a few entries with no hypothesis line are built from the header text and may hint at the setting; the oracle is one campaign on one project; the analyst's claim-state rule is the same prompt in every arm, so the ablation isolates planner context only. Scripts: avrl/exp/ar1_loop.py, avrl/exp/ar1_analyze.py, avrl/exp/ar1_run_all.sh.

## AR2 2026-10-16 23:55 UTC pre-registered (offline evaluation of the critic role, built from the ledger; running)
Hypothesis (from autoresearch-routing-context.md, upgrade 1): the critic is where model quality buys the most, because one wrong number poisons the ledger. Test: does a stronger model catch more planted defects in ledger entries, and at what false-alarm cost?
Substrate: the 52 small-scale entries (real critic notes removed). For each entry with a table: the untouched entry (control); a copy with one narrative number that also appears in the table changed by 20 to 60 percent (number defect); a copy with the Verdict paragraph's verdict word flipped (verdict defect). The critic sees the entry alone, no raw files, and must say whether it is internally consistent and quote the offending text. Models: Haiku and Sonnet, same prompt. About 150 items x 2 models.
Metrics: detection rate (flagged) and location rate (quote or problem names the planted value, or the verdict) per defect kind; false-alarm rate on controls; cost.
Pre-registered reads: Read 1 PASS if Sonnet's located rate exceeds Haiku's by at least 0.15 on the number defects with a false-alarm rate no more than 0.10 higher. Read 2: verdict flips should be the easier defect (located rate above 0.7 for both models); if not, the entries do not state their thresholds clearly enough for an entry-only critic, which is a finding about the ledger format.
Limits: entry-only critique is a weaker task than the loop's real critic (which re-derives numbers from raw files); the planted defects are synthetic; one seed of planting. Script: avrl/exp/ar2_critic_eval.py; results avrl/runs/AR2/.

## AR1 2026-10-17 00:40 UTC kill (both pre-registered reads not met: full logs beat the compressed ledger, and inspirations hurt)
Run as pre-registered: 3 arms x 5 seeds, Sonnet planner and analyst, 52-experiment oracle, cap 20 iterations. No run was censored; every run ended on the correct claim state (claim 1 pass, claim 2 kill or tie, claim 3 kill). Files: avrl/runs/AR1/<arm>_s<seed>/{trace.jsonl,result.json}, avrl/runs/AR1/result.json.

| arm (planner context) | experiments to verdict, mean [per seed] | claim flips | cost per run USD | planner input tokens per run | analyst agreement with recorded verdicts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A full logs | 5.2 [9, 6, 2, 3, 6] | 2 | 0.38 | 59k | 0.72 |
| B compressed ledger | 7.2 [4, 12, 7, 3, 10] | 0 | 0.44 | 63k | 0.73 |
| C compressed plus inspirations | 10.2 [7, 16, 9, 6, 13] | 1 | 0.63 | 98k | 0.60 |

Seed-paired: B needs fewer experiments than A in 1 of 5 seeds (1 tie); C fewer than A in 1 of 5; C fewer than B in 0 of 5.
Verdict: Read 1 (B or C beats A on experiments-to-verdict at lower cost) NOT MET; Read 2 (C beats B) NOT MET. Kill for the two "cheapest upgrades" proposed in autoresearch-routing-context.md, on this substrate.
Reading: the full-log planner reaches the right verdicts fastest and, because it stops earlier, is also the cheapest per run; token savings from compression never offset the extra iterations. Inspirations (two full entries of killed experiments) made things worse on every seed against B: the planner re-spent picks on the neighbourhood of the dead ends it had just been shown (traces show repeated replay-bootstrap picks such as L13a, L12b, L10a after seeing a killed replay entry), which is the opposite of the OpenEvolve rationale. Across all 15 runs the planner's first pick was the in-training kill test P1-goldberg-s0 in 10 of 15 cases, so the menu is informative enough that a good planner settles claims 2 and 3 in a handful of picks; the spread across seeds (2 to 16) comes mostly from how soon it picks a claim 1 reference arm (M13, M14).
Caveats: one project, one campaign, 5 seeds, no interval; the oracle replays a finished campaign, so the planner cannot propose anything new and experiments-to-verdict rewards recognising known decisive experiments; a few menu lines hint at the setting; same analyst prompt in every arm (arm C's lower agreement reflects which experiments it chose, not a different analyst). Not tested: the critic-model upgrade (AR2, running).
Consequence for the write-up: autoresearch-routing-context.md upgrade 2 (inspirations) is withdrawn pending a live test; the recommendation becomes "give the planner the full ledger while it fits the context window, and compress only when it does not".
Critic note (self-check against the traces, 00:50 UTC): the sentence attributing arm C's extra picks to "the neighbourhood of the dead ends it had just been shown" overreaches. The orders show arm C's long runs spending picks on experiments that cannot move a claim (the three burn-in auto-budget runs E10e, E10e-rel, E10e-up in C seed 1; replay bootstraps L9c, L9d, L12b, L13a in C seed 4), and arm B's long runs do the same (L13a, L12b, L10a in B seed 1). Whether the inspirations caused the extra low-value picks is not identifiable from five seeds; what is supported is only that C never beat B. Verdict unchanged.

## AR2 2026-10-17 01:05 UTC inconclusive (Read 1 not met, Read 2 met; the stronger critic buys fewer false alarms, not more detections)
Run as pre-registered: 138 items (49 controls, 47 number defects, 42 verdict flips) x Haiku and Sonnet, entry-only critique, seed 0 planting, 276 calls, no failed calls. Files: avrl/runs/AR2/{rows.jsonl,result.json}.

| model | number defect flagged / located | verdict flip flagged / located | false alarms on controls | false alarms on clean controls (n=28) | cost USD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Haiku | 0.94 / 0.72 | 0.93 / 0.83 | 0.57 | 0.50 | 0.20 |
| Sonnet | 0.89 / 0.81 | 0.81 / 0.74 | 0.31 | 0.21 | 2.40 |

Verdict: Read 1 (Sonnet located rate at least 0.15 above Haiku on number defects, false alarms no more than 0.10 higher) NOT MET: the gap is +0.09 (0.81 vs 0.72; on the clean subset 0.77 vs 0.73). Read 2 (verdict flips located above 0.7 by both) MET: 0.83 and 0.74 (clean subset 0.86 and 0.82), so the entries state their thresholds well enough for an entry-only critic to catch a flipped verdict.
Reading: the two models detect planted defects at similar rates; the difference is specificity. Haiku calls half of the untouched entries inconsistent, Sonnet one in five, at twelve times the cost. For a loop whose critic appends a correction note that a human then reads, false alarms are the expensive failure (every note costs attention and erodes trust in the ledger), so the case for the stronger model on the critic role rests on precision, not recall. The routing write-up's upgrade 1 is supported in that narrower form.
Measurement caveat found during analysis: the controls are not all clean. Twenty-one of the 52 entries carry a real correction inside the analyst's own text (a "Corrected verdict" paragraph, a review note, a stated wrong number), and both models correctly flagged several of those (E04's 19-versus-17 count, L5a's 10,035-versus-10,036 count), which my scoring counts as false alarms. The clean-subset column (31 entries with no such text) is the better estimate of the false-alarm rate; the direction and size of the Haiku-Sonnet gap are the same on both. Real inconsistencies the models found in untouched entries are also a small by-product: an entry-only critic re-reading the ledger surfaces internal contradictions the file-based critic left in the prose.
Other limits: entry-only critique is weaker than the loop's real critic (which re-derives numbers from raw files); defects are synthetic and one planting seed; located-rate scoring is a string match on the planted value or the word "verdict", so a correct diagnosis phrased differently counts as a miss (Haiku 10 and Sonnet 4 of the number misses were flagged but with another location).
Consequence: the two experiments together say: keep the full ledger in the planner's context (AR1), and if one role gets a stronger model, make it the critic, for precision (AR2). A live test on real experiments, with the file-based critic, is the next step and is not run.

## AR3 2026-10-17 19:16 UTC KILL (critic cascade: agreement among Haiku passes does not identify the entries worth escalating; the burn-in diagnostic predicted it)
Run as pre-registered (PREREG.md and the two freeze entries above). 64 pool entries, 328 items, 2,624 calls (5 Haiku and 3 Sonnet passes per item), 0 failed calls, total $15.36 (Haiku $0.0013 and Sonnet $0.0121 per call). Blind adjudication of every control flagged by a majority of either model: 18 burn and 53 test review rows; 9 of 16 burn and 14 of 48 test controls carried a real contradiction and moved to the natural stratum (labeled files runs/AR3/review_pile_*_labeled.csv, labels runs/AR3/control_labels.json). Test slice: 48 entries, 224 items (34 clean controls, 47 N-big, 70 N-mid, 41 N-stale, 17 V-body, 15 V-coh), prevalence weights pi=0.25, effective sample size 55. Frozen operating point e=0.25 (realized weighted e_hat 0.279, Wilson [0.18, 0.41]). Files: runs/AR3/result.json, table.md, fig/, cascade_config.json.

| policy (test slice, weighted quality Q, 1.0 = every control unflagged and every plant located) | Q | strong-call fraction | dollar cost vs all-Sonnet |
|---|---|---|---|
| all-Haiku, 1 pass | 0.323 | 0 | 0.11 |
| all-Haiku, 3 passes majority (P0b) | 0.370 | 0 | 0.33 |
| all-Haiku, 5 passes majority | 0.371 | 0 | 0.54 |
| cascade, agreement threshold, frozen (P3) | 0.462 | 0.279 | 0.61 |
| random escalation at the same rate | 0.471 | 0.279 | 0.61 |
| cascade, verbalized confidence k=3 (P4b) at e=0.25 | 0.531 | 0.25 | 0.58 |
| cascade, verbalized confidence 1 pass (P4a) at e=0.25 | 0.517 | 0.25 | 0.36 |
| learned logistic scorer (P7, burn-in trained) at e=0.25 | 0.499 | 0.25 | 0.58 |
| escalate on any Haiku flag (P5) | 0.748 | 0.834 | 1.16 |
| all-Sonnet, single pass (P1, comparator) | 0.734 | 1.0 | 1.0 |
| all-Sonnet, majority of 3 | 0.749 | 3.0 | 3.0 |

Gates: G0 pass (Sonnet macro located recall 0.902; Sonnet false alarms 0.314 on 34 adjudicated clean controls; 100 percent valid calls). G1 pass (Q_S minus Q_H3 = +0.364, 90 percent CI [+0.256, +0.471]).
R1 (cascade beats random at e_hat): FAIL. Q_C minus Q_rand = -0.009, one-sided 95 percent lower bound -0.059, sign-flip p 0.59; q = 0.01 (unresolvable at this n, MDE 0.096), so R1 is "unresolved" in the table and fails the read.
R2 (non-inferior to Sonnet within 0.05): FAIL. Q_S minus Q_C = +0.272, 90 percent CI [+0.181, +0.368]; the one-sided lower bound exceeds the margin, so K2 fires: KILL. K1 (gap closed) unresolved (-0.05, 90 percent CI [-0.35, +0.27]). Qualifier: no plant kind has a confident-wrong rate of 0.50 or more; the confident-wrong mass is all on controls (0.35 unconditional, 0.86 given full agreement).
Secondary (Holm, m=3): S1 AUROC(conf_agree) minus AUROC(conf_verb) for Haiku-vs-Sonnet disagreement = -0.280 (0.578 vs 0.857), 90 percent CI [-0.44, -0.11], the opposite sign: not supported. S2 learned scorer minus threshold at e=0.25 = +0.054, upper bound +0.083: unresolved (not shown to be under 0.02). S3 cascade minus all-Haiku k=5 at equal dollars = +0.052, lower bound +0.003, Holm p 0.14: unresolved.
Diagnostic: diagnose's recommended budget b* = 0.75 (burn-in; test AUROC of conf_agree for Haiku-vs-Sonnet disagreement 0.578, regime label uninformative; threshold error at 25 percent budget 0.308 against random 0.302). At b* the cascade escalates 0.79 and reaches Q 0.684. The agreement curve needs e = 0.94 to reach 98 percent of Q_S; verbalized confidence needs 0.54 (k=3) or 0.69 (one pass).
Exploratory: per-kind located recall, Haiku k=3: N-big 0.94, N-mid 0.91, N-stale 0.93, V-body 1.00, V-coh 0.93; Sonnet single pass: 0.82, 0.84, 0.91, 0.94, 1.00. Clean controls left unflagged: Haiku k=3 0.21, Sonnet 0.69. Natural probes (6 entries with historical critic corrections): Haiku flags 83 percent of passes and locates the real defect in 63 percent; Sonnet 50 and 39 percent. Oracle versus extra stratum: Q_H3 0.46 on critic-verified entries against 0.23 on the uncritiqued ones (Haiku flags the main-session entries more). A/A noise floor: Sonnet pass pairs differ by up to 0.09 in Q (95th percentile of the paired bootstrap 0.15). 1,000 random entry splits: b* 5th to 95th percentile 0.45 to 0.85, Q_S minus Q_C 0.15 to 0.35, share of splits meeting non-inferiority 0.001. pi sensitivity (0.10, 0.50) and M sensitivity (0.03, 0.08): R2 kill at every setting. Item-level McNemar Haiku k=3 vs Sonnet majority: n10 13, n01 19, mid-p 0.30 (unweighted, exploratory).
Reading. The analogue of RL claim 1 fails at the critic role, and the failure is informative. The cheap critic is not weaker at finding defects; it is worse at leaving clean entries alone, and its false alarms are systematic: the three passes agree with each other while being wrong, so agreement has nothing to say about which entries need the strong model. In RL the cheap grader's errors were stochastic (split votes), which is exactly when a consistency threshold works. The burn-in diagnostic, run on 16 entries before any test row was opened, recommended a 75 percent budget and labelled the signal uninformative, which is the right call: the thesis's regime detector transfers even though the thesis's rule does not. The signal that does carry information here is the one that was useless in RL, the cheap model's verbalized confidence (AUROC 0.86 vs 0.58), and a one-pass verbalized cascade at e=0.25 reaches Q 0.52 at 36 percent of Sonnet's cost; still far below Sonnet (0.73), so there is no cheap route to Sonnet-level critique in this setting. Escalating on any Haiku flag recovers Sonnet quality (0.75) but at 116 percent of Sonnet's cost.
Side finding on the ledger itself: 23 of the 64 pool entries (36 percent) carried a real internal contradiction between their prose and their own table or thresholds that the day-one file-based critic had not caught, including a reversed pass-condition sentence in L3a, "within 2 points" verdicts with 3-point gaps (E10b, E10e-rel), and a "never worse than any learned allocator" claim beside a table where it is beaten. These are now listed in runs/AR3/review_pile_*_labeled.csv for correction in a separate pass; no ledger entry was edited.
Deviations (all recorded in PREREG.md before the test rows were opened): one adjudication correction (M08a), the rubric disclosure (unsupported-but-not-contradicted overclaims count as false alarms for both models), and three analysis-time items listed in result.json (Haiku passes 4 and 5 absent for the 29 probe-stratum items, A/A floor for Sonnet pairs only, tolerance sensitivity not run).
Consequence for the thesis write-up: the critic-cascade analogue is a documented negative with a mechanism (systematic versus stochastic cheap-grader error) and a working regime detector. For the Zubin write-up: lead with the burn-in prediction and the agreement-versus-verbalized reversal.

## AR5 2026-10-18 23:01 UTC pre-registered (live routed autoresearch loop, one 8-hour block, five arms; launch pending)

Identical allow-listed starting state (ledger with E01 only, PROJECT.md claims, backlog E01 to G04 with host names removed and the GRPO infeasibility note, E01 rollout assets, GSM8K data, four small models, aa/data.py). Ground truth: day-one claim 2 not supported and claim 3 killed, with the right-reason rubric and the machine gate on real result files; guessability control 5 of 5 (recorded), so evidence production is what is measured. Predictions: CASCADE escalation 0.50 to 0.80; CHEAP highest residual error; FULL settles in 8 to 12 experiments under 200 minutes; ROUTED cost 0.25 to 0.35 of FULL; cascade saving over ROUTED 10 to 30 percent; the RL "quarter of the calls" result not expected to transfer. Reads R-reach, R-time, R-cost, R-error and the R-noise gate as in PREREG.md section 7. Pilot (unscored, P0TEST on cherry, 2026-10-18): one full iteration through Agent calls verified planner, runner, analyst, two screeners, Sonnet critic and GPU dispatch; a Workflow-tool pilot then ran its gate agent, which stopped correctly on a stale deadline. The block launch line with T0 and the resolved model ids follows this entry when the arms start.

Launch line (2026-10-18 23:15 UTC): run trees created from the patched seed at 23:11 to 23:15 UTC, leak gate clean on all five, pre-warm 30 to 137 s per host; snapshotter started; T0 = 1792365340 (2026-10-18 23:15:40 UTC) written to each BASE/T0; gate deadline T0 + 7 h 15 min (06:30 UTC), hard stop T0 + 8 h 30 min (07:45 UTC); five arms launched through the Workflow tool with args per avrl/exp/AR5_LAUNCH.md (max_iters 40, stop_after_s 26100). ## AR5 2026-10-19 08:40 UTC result: UNRESOLVED by the pre-registered noise gate (the two all-Sonnet runs disagree), reported as a variance measurement of autoresearch loops
Block ran 2026-10-18 23:15:40 UTC to 2026-10-19 06:58 UTC; every arm stopped by the gate at the 7 h 15 min deadline; 466 agents, 7,634 tool uses, 60.0 M subagent tokens; resolved model ids claude-sonnet-5-5 and claude-haiku-5-5, 466 agent-model pairs checked, 0 mismatches. Originals untouched (only this ledger changed, by the orchestrator's appends); every run tree's frozen files verified (17 of 17 per tree); GPUs clear at the end. Files: avrl/runs/AR5/{grades_<run>.json, cost.json, cost.log, leak_scan.json, trace_<run>.json, audit/, prefixes/, <run>/ (copied trees without weights)}.

| arm (roles) | host | experiments | first V | settled k* | wall-clock to k* | cost to k* (USD list) | total cost | final claim state (adjudicator) | ever wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FULL (all Sonnet) | cherry | 12 | 9 | 11 | 398 min | 40.2 | 45.2 | c2 not supported, c3 killed | yes (c3 "supported" at k=5 to 8 and 10) |
| FULL_R (all Sonnet) | spruce | 18 | 2 | never | n/a | n/a | 55.8 | c2 SUPPORTED, c3 killed | yes, final state wrong vs the key |
| ROUTED (Haiku roles, Sonnet critic) | willow | 21 | never | never | n/a | n/a | 16.2 | c2 not supported, c3 open | no |
| CHEAP (all Haiku) | walnut | 16 | never | never | n/a | n/a | 2.2 | c2 not supported, c3 open | yes (c2 "supported" at k=9) |
| CASCADE (Haiku roles, 2 Haiku screeners, Sonnet critic on flag) | pine | 18 | 5 | 5 | 82 min | 2.6 | 11.9 | c2 not supported, c3 killed | no |
| day-one reference (all Sonnet 5, steered) | A4000 | 8 | 7 | 7 | 142 min | about 15 | 17.7 | c2 not supported, c3 killed | E03 trap, repaired by L3a |

Verdict reached = machine gate (two real non-E01 result files) plus a blind three-pass Sonnet adjudicator with the right-reason rubric (claim 2 not supported only with a budget-matched comparison and no information advantage; claim 3 killed only with a non-E01 point), majority of three, settled when it holds on two consecutive prefixes. The adjudicator failed on prefixes above 128 KB in the first pass (prompt passed as a command-line argument); the prompt was moved to stdin and every prefix re-adjudicated (cache for the first four prefixes per arm); adjudication cost 82 USD in total.
R-noise gate: TRIGGERED. The two all-Sonnet runs disagree on reach (FULL settled at k=11; FULL_R first reached the key's state at k=2, then declared claim 2 supported from k=3 and held it to the end). By the pre-registration every arm contrast is therefore "unresolved" and AR5 is a measurement of run-to-run variance. Descriptive reads below are labelled as such.
R-reach (descriptive): CASCADE and FULL reached and held the key's verdicts; ROUTED and CHEAP never did (21 and 16 experiments, all on the E01 rollouts: neither Haiku planner ever ran a second task or proxy, so claim 3 stayed open); FULL_R ended in a state the key calls wrong. R-time (descriptive): CASCADE k* = 5 at 82 min against the day-one 7 at 142 min; FULL k* = 11 at 398 min. R-cost (descriptive): ROUTED total 0.32 of the FULL mean (50.5), inside the predicted 0.25 to 0.35; CASCADE escalated on 18 of 18 iterations (every screener pair flagged), so the cascade cost claim is KILLED by its own pre-registered rule (escalation above 0.80); CASCADE's lower total than ROUTED (11.9 vs 16.2, 0.66 vs 0.77 per iteration) comes from cheaper critic calls, not from skipped ones. Prediction P-a (escalation 0.50 to 0.80) missed high, P-d (FULL settles in 8 to 12 experiments under 200 min) met on count and missed on time, P-e met, P-f met in total but for the wrong reason.
R-error (descriptive; validity gate G-aud FAILED so no "supported" read): the Sonnet auditor (entry text plus cited result.json, run.log tail and script; taxonomy V, N, T, U, F; MATERIAL = V or N or F) flags MATERIAL errors in 1 of 12 FULL, 1 of 18 FULL_R, 0 of 21 ROUTED, 1 of 16 CHEAP and 0 of 18 CASCADE experiments; narrative-number (T) and unsupported-claim (U) flags are common in every arm (T 4 to 15, U 9 to 23 per arm). Validation: on the five seeded day-one defects the auditor flagged 4 of 5 but matched the registered kind on only 2 of 5 (E03 and L7b; it missed E04's two wrong numbers and L5a's off-by-one count); on planted twins it caught 5 of 6 flipped verdicts and 5 of 9 changed numbers, with 0 of 14 twin controls flagged material. The five day-one "clean" controls were all flagged, and three of those flags reproduce real contradictions AR3 had found blind (L3a's reversed pass condition, L6a's 50 percent budget, E06's "further below 1.0", E08's 106 versus 138). Error rates are therefore descriptive only. The no-model trace cross-check: fraction of numbers traceable to the cited raw files FULL 0.78, FULL_R 0.96, ROUTED 0.86, CHEAP 0.68, CASCADE 0.74; mismatched numbers 1 to 4 percent per arm.
Critics: the Sonnet critic appended a correction in 11 of 12 FULL entries, 17 of 18 FULL_R, 21 of 21 ROUTED and 18 of 18 CASCADE; the Haiku critic in 15 of 16 CHEAP. Correction is the norm, not the exception, for every critic model.
Leak scan (per-arm, transcripts): hard hits CASCADE 4, FULL_R 1, others 0. On inspection: two CASCADE hits are grep patterns that contain host names (searching bin/gpu_run.sh for the word ssh), one is a listing of a path that does not exist, and one is a critic listing the file names of the originals' lit/avrl directory (names only, no content), which the pre-registration classes as a soft incident; FULL_R's hit is a read of the harness's own tool-results spill (exempt by the pre-run decision); FULL's 35 soft "S1 " hits are its own "S1 null" sensitivity label. No arm is classed CONTAMINATED; all hits are disclosed in avrl/runs/AR5/leak_scan.json.
Reading. (1) Run-to-run variance of a Sonnet autoresearch loop from an identical seed is large: one copy reached the campaign's verdicts in 11 experiments, the other copy spent 18 experiments building a case that claim 2 is supported (a tuned learned allocator beating its fixed comparator in its own replay framework: L2a, L6a, L7a, L7b, L13a, L15a, L8a all pass) and ended in disagreement with the reference. Whether FULL_R is wrong or the day-one reference is, needs Rustom's adjudication of those entries, as pre-registered. (2) Planner choice dominated everything: the only arms that settled were the ones whose planner left the E01 rollouts for a second task or proxy (E04 in CASCADE at iteration 5, E04 first in FULL_R, the judge-proxy and fresh-shard probes in FULL); the two Haiku arms that stayed on E01 could not kill claim 3 no matter how many experiments they ran. CASCADE's fast settle is a planner outcome, since its critic path was identical to ROUTED's (escalation 1.0). (3) The cascade signal failed exactly as AR2 and AR3 predicted: Haiku screeners flag every entry, so flag-or-disagree escalation is always-escalate. (4) Material errors in the final, critic-corrected text are rare in every arm, including all-Haiku; the model gap shows up in narrative-number and overclaim flags and in critic yield, not in verdict or table errors. (5) Cost: Haiku roles cut the bill to a third (ROUTED) or a twentieth (CHEAP) of all-Sonnet, but with n = 1 per Haiku arm and the noise gate triggered, no claim about equal quality at that cost is supported; the honest statement is that whether a routed loop reaches the right verdict depended here on one planner decision, not on the critic model.
Consequence for the thesis analogy: the live loop did not give a clean test of routing; it gave a variance measurement and a mechanism. In RL the cheap grader's errors were per-group and partly random; in the loop the decisive variation is which experiment the planner picks, a single high-leverage choice per iteration that no verifier role can repair. Next (not launched): replicate FULL two more times to measure the Sonnet variance floor before any routing contrast, and give every arm a fixed first experiment (E04) so planner luck on claim 3 is removed from the comparison.

## AR6 pre-registered (planner routing policies in the autoresearch loop, replayed offline; running)
Question: in an autoresearch loop, when should the planner's pick be made by the strong model? AR5 showed the planner's choice is the high-leverage decision and that cheap screeners flag everything, so critic cascades do not save calls. The literature routes per query (RouteLLM, FrugalGPT cascades, Dekoninck et al. unified routing and cascading) or per step by estimated failure risk (R2V-Agent); none targets the planner of a research loop or uses progress as the signal.
Substrate: the AR1 oracle (52 critic-checked experiments, lookup runner, fixed Sonnet analyst tracking the three claims, planner sees full logs, the best context per AR1). Ground truth: claim 1 pass, claim 2 kill or tie, claim 3 kill; settled when matched on two consecutive iterations; cap 20.
Arms (planner only differs): HAIKU; SONNET; CONSIST3 (three Haiku samples, escalate to Sonnet unless two agree; the thesis's consistency rule on the planner); VERBAL (Haiku verbalized confidence, escalate below 70); STALL (new: Haiku by default, escalate to Sonnet after two consecutive iterations without a claim-state change, then back to Haiku; progress-triggered escalation); STRONG_EARLY (Sonnet while any claim is open, Haiku after); RANDOM25 (Haiku, Sonnet with probability 0.25; budget control). Ten seeds per arm (menu order and random draws). Second block, same arms with an open-weights model (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ served by vLLM on a lab A4000, and Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct on an A100 if the tunnel holds) as the cheap tier instead of Haiku, so the cheap-versus-frontier routing case is covered.
Metrics: experiments to verdict (primary), strong-planner-call fraction and cost (secondary), claim flips, censoring.
Pre-registered reads (means over ten seeds, seed-paired sign counts, bootstrap intervals; n is small, so each read is also reported with its minimum detectable effect): R1 (routing is needed): HAIKU is at least one experiment slower than SONNET. R2 (claim 1 analogue): at least one of CONSIST3, VERBAL, STALL is within one experiment of SONNET while making at most 40 percent of its picks with Sonnet. R3 (the improvement): STALL is at least as fast as CONSIST3 and VERBAL at an equal or lower strong-pick fraction. R4: RANDOM25 is slower than the best cascade at a matched strong-pick fraction (routing beats luck). Predictions: HAIKU slower by 2 to 4 experiments; CONSIST3 escalates often because plan picks disagree more than critic flags do; STALL uses Sonnet on 15 to 30 percent of picks. Open-model block reads are the same with "Haiku" replaced by the open model. Scripts avrl/exp/ar6_route_loop.py, results avrl/runs/AR6/.

## AR6 result: R1 and R4 pass, R2 and R3 fail; the unregistered strong-early policy beats the all-Sonnet planner in both blocks
Block 1 (Haiku cheap tier, Sonnet strong tier, Sonnet analyst): 7 policies x 10 seeds, all complete. Block 2 (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ served by vLLM on a lab A4000 as the cheap tier): 7 policies x 5 seeds, all complete. Block 3 (Qwen2.5-14B on an A100) NOT run: the server came up on the hannah compute node but its port is unreachable from outside the node and the reverse tunnel needed an SSH key added to authorized_keys, which the harness refused as a persistence change; recorded as not run. Files: avrl/runs/AR6/<policy>_s<seed>/, open7b_<policy>_s<seed>/, result_haiku.json, result_open7b.json.

| planner policy | Haiku tier: experiments to verdict, mean [90 percent CI] | strong picks | Qwen-7B tier: mean [90 percent CI] | strong picks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fixed cheap | 12.9 [10.7, 15.2], 1 censored | 0.00 | 17.8 [13.2, 21.0], 3 censored | 0.00 |
| fixed Sonnet | 7.5 [5.2, 10.0] | 1.00 | 7.6 [6.0, 9.4] | 1.00 |
| CONSIST3 (3 cheap samples, escalate on disagreement) | 7.9 [5.6, 10.7] | 0.51 | 11.6 [10.0, 13.2] | 0.20 |
| VERBAL (cheap confidence under 70 escalates) | 8.6 [5.5, 12.1], 2 censored | 1.00 | 18.8 [16.6, 21.0], 3 censored | 0.00 |
| STALL (escalate after two stalled iterations) | 13.8 [11.1, 16.4] | 0.20 | 12.2 [10.0, 14.4] | 0.30 |
| STRONG_EARLY (Sonnet while any claim open) | 6.9 [5.8, 8.0] | 0.86 | 5.0 [4.0, 6.0] | 0.82 |
| RANDOM25 | 11.5 [8.6, 14.6], 2 censored | 0.27 | 15.6 [11.4, 19.8], 2 censored | 0.21 |

Seed-paired (mean difference in experiments, 90 percent bootstrap CI): Haiku tier: cheap minus Sonnet +5.4 [+3.0, +7.7] (Sonnet fewer in 9 of 10); CONSIST3 minus Sonnet +0.4 [-2.7, +3.9]; STRONG_EARLY minus Sonnet -0.6 [-2.3, +0.8]; STALL minus CONSIST3 +5.9 [+2.3, +9.7]; CONSIST3 minus RANDOM25 -3.6 [-7.4, 0.0]. Qwen-7B tier: cheap minus Sonnet +10.2 [+3.6, +14.4]; CONSIST3 minus Sonnet +4.0 [+0.6, +6.8]; STRONG_EARLY minus Sonnet -2.6 [-3.8, -1.4] (fewer in 5 of 5); CONSIST3 minus RANDOM25 -4.0 [-6.6, -1.4] at a matched strong-pick fraction (0.20 vs 0.21); STALL minus CONSIST3 +0.6 [-0.8, +1.8].
Reads. R1 (cheap planner slower by at least one experiment): PASS in both blocks (5.4 and 10.2 experiments slower). R2 (a cascade within one experiment of Sonnet at no more than 40 percent Sonnet picks): FAIL in both; CONSIST3 is within one experiment of Sonnet on the Haiku tier but spends 51 percent of picks on Sonnet, and on the Qwen tier no cascade is within one experiment. R3 (STALL at least as fast as CONSIST3 and VERBAL at equal or lower strong fraction): FAIL; on the Haiku tier STALL is 5.9 experiments slower than CONSIST3 and no faster than the cheap planner alone; on the Qwen tier it ties CONSIST3 at a higher strong fraction. R4 (random escalation slower than the best cascade): PASS in both; on the Qwen tier at a matched fraction with the interval excluding zero, on the Haiku tier at an unmatched fraction (0.51 vs 0.27), so only the Qwen-tier comparison is clean. Predictions: HAIKU slower by 2 to 4 was too optimistic (5.4); CONSIST3 escalating often held on the Haiku tier (51 percent) but not on the Qwen tier (20 percent, the open model's three samples agree more); STALL at 15 to 30 percent strong picks held (20 and 30) but the policy did not work.
Reading. (1) The planner is worth paying for: a cheap planner costs five to ten extra experiments on this replay, and the open 7B model mostly cannot find the decisive experiments at all (three of five runs censored at 20). (2) Consistency among cheap samples IS an informative signal for the planner, the reverse of AR3's finding for the critic: plan picks disagree stochastically where critic flags were unanimous, so disagreement marks the picks worth escalating, and CONSIST3 beats random escalation at a matched budget by four experiments on the Qwen tier. It is not cheap enough to meet the pre-registered 40 percent bar on the Haiku tier, where Haiku's samples disagree half the time. (3) Progress-triggered escalation (STALL, the proposed improvement) fails: by the time two iterations have stalled the loop has already spent its cheap picks on low-value experiments, and one strong pick does not recover; escalation should be decided before the pick, not after the damage. (4) Verbalized confidence is unusable as written: Haiku is never above 70 (always escalates, so VERBAL equals Sonnet at full cost) and the open 7B model is never below 70 (never escalates); a threshold set by burn-in rather than fixed would be needed, and the signal's calibration differs by model, which AR3 also found. (5) The unregistered STRONG_EARLY policy is the empirical winner in both blocks and beats the all-Sonnet planner in 5 of 5 seeds on the Qwen tier (5.0 vs 7.6) at 82 percent Sonnet picks: use the strong model while any claim is open (the exploration phase, where the pick decides which claims can be settled) and the cheap model once every claim has a state (the confirmation phase, where a conservative pick that does not reopen a claim is what settles the run; Sonnet's own picks in that phase reopen claims more often, 3 flips against 1 on the Haiku tier). Because this read was not pre-registered it is a hypothesis for replication, not a result; the saving is also modest (14 to 18 percent of planner picks), so its value is in speed, not cost.
Improvement over the literature, stated carefully: per-step risk routing (R2V-Agent) and unified cascade routing (Dekoninck et al.) both require a trained quality or risk estimator per step. In a research loop the state of the pre-registered claims is a free, exact phase signal: open claims mean the next pick is high-leverage, settled claims mean it is not. STRONG_EARLY routes on that signal with no estimator and no extra calls, and on this replay it is faster than always using the strong model. CONSIST3 is the estimator-free fallback when no claim structure exists.
Deviations: (a) the first launch queued runs policy-major; after 6 completions it was restarted seed-major so a deadline cut would be balanced, and the 6 finished runs were kept; (b) three Haiku-tier runs failed on a headless-call error (the model attempted a tool call with tools disabled) and were rerun (HAIKU s5, STALL s3, VERBAL s4); (c) four Qwen-tier runs exceeded the served 24k-token context on long histories and failed with HTTP 400; a truncation rule (drop the oldest half of the run history and retry) was added to the open-model call and those runs were rerun, so late Qwen-tier iterations saw a shortened history; (d) block 3 not run (above); (e) the VERBAL threshold of 70 was fixed in advance and turned out degenerate for both tiers. Costs: list-price equivalent per run 0.28 to 0.68 USD on the Claude plan; the open model ran free on the lab GPU; about 110 runs, roughly 45 USD list-price in total, zero cash. Limits: one substrate (the ledger replay, known menu), ten and five seeds, intervals wide; STRONG_EARLY untested in a live loop where claims can be wrongly closed early.
